<div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-323"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="323" data-folio="231" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n322_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 231">[p. 231]</button></div><h2 class="sec-h1">What łres were written from his ma<sup>tie</sup> to the Princes in the East Indies in ffebruarie 1607.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-001" href="#sec-07-fn-001" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-001" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></h2><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">In all 12 łres were now sent in Spaignish as before.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-003" href="#sec-07-fn-003" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-003" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup></aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">That</span> was the form<sup>r</sup> 7 łres <em>folio.</em> 41.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-002" href="#sec-07-fn-002" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-002" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Viz to Suratt Aden Tedore Nere Lunter Iava Maior, Molloccos, &amp; the łre of the Teno<sup>r</sup> of that to Surate purposed for Callicutt, written from his ma<sup>tie</sup>: to those Princes Anno 1606. were now all againe of new written from his ma<sup>tie</sup>: to those Princes in Anno 1607 w<sup>th</sup> this Addic̃on to each onelie That is. Although we sent vnto you the last yeare after the retourne of o<sup>r</sup> Subiectę from those pte &amp; sent you a Present yet for that we doubt whether those łres be come to yo<sup>r</sup> hande, we haue thought good to Visitt you againe w<sup>th</sup> new łres &amp; w<sup>th</sup> a new present, And to lett you vnderstand that the Contente of o<sup>r</sup> former łres were to this purpose That &amp;c: as in the m<sup>r</sup>gent of the foresaid łre to the Molloccos.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-004" href="#sec-07-fn-004" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-004" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Lykewise ffower łres more were now written from his ma<sup>tie</sup>: to the Princes &amp; Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> in those parts Viz 2 of the Teno<sup>r</sup> of that of Suratt w<sup>th</sup> generall salutačons in the inside &amp; on the outside left Blancke for o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> to sett downe the names and titles of such Princes there as they shall see good to deliu<sup>r</sup> them vnto, w<sup>th</sup> Presentę alsoe in their discrec̃ons. Thother 2 łres from his ma<sup>tie</sup>: to Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> of Citties, Viz the one of w<sup>ch</sup> of the Teno<sup>r</sup> of that to Aden w<sup>th</sup>out the said new Addic̃on before mencioned intended to the Sabander or Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of Moccha &amp; thother alsoe of the same Teno<sup>r</sup> of that to Aden onelie leaueing out the said new addic̃on now sent, &amp; the wordę of his ma<sup>ties</sup> Amitie w<sup>th</sup> the Grandsenio<sup>r</sup> mencioned in the said first łre to Aden the w<sup>ch</sup> is intended for Lawrie<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-005" href="#sec-07-fn-005" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-005" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> Nigertattie<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-006" href="#sec-07-fn-006" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-006" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> or any such place &amp; therefore the supscripc̃on left vnsett downe, vntill the same be knowen vnto whome they shall be thought fitt to be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed vnto in those parte.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-324"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="324" data-folio="232" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n323_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 232">[p. 232]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ffor better Remembrance to direct the titles of the before mencioned 5 blancke 3 whereof to Princes &amp; 2 to Citties they are nombred as followeth Viz.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">N<sup>o</sup> 1 is the Coppie of the first blanck sent in the yeare 1606 w<sup>th</sup> the new Addic̃on now onelie to be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to Callicutt or to such Prince as you shall vnderstand, the same was then form<sup>r</sup>lie deliu<sup>r</sup>ed</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">No 2 &amp; 3 is 2 blancke of the Teno<sup>r</sup> of that to Suratt to any Prince vnto whome form<sup>r</sup>lie noe łres hath beene sent &amp; therefore w<sup>th</sup>out the said Addic̃on No 4 is the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of a Cittie of the teno<sup>r</sup> of that to Aden w<sup>th</sup>out any Addic̃on for that noe łres hath beene heretofore sent thither, the w<sup>ch</sup> is intended for Moccha some 10 miles from Aden vnder the Comand of the Grandsenio<sup>r</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">No 5 The 5 &amp; last Blancke alsoe to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of some Cittie of the same teno<sup>r</sup> of that to Aden w<sup>th</sup>out any Addic̃on for the reason aforesaid in no: 4 &amp; w<sup>th</sup>out any nominac̃on of o<sup>r</sup> kinge ma<sup>ties</sup>: Amitie w<sup>th</sup> the grandsenio<sup>r</sup> for that the same is intended for some Cittie out of his Dominions.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Lres of safe Condut <sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-008" href="#sec-07-fn-008" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-008" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> for Iohn Iourdan &amp; Phillip Glascocke &amp;c: goeing to the East Indies . 1607</aside></div><p class="prose-p">JAMES by the grace of God of greate Brittaine ffraunce and Ireland Kinge Defendo<sup>r</sup> of the faith &amp;c: To all and eu<sup>r</sup>y one Kinge, Princes, Duke, Marquesses State Admiralle Captaines Castellers Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> and Superio<sup>rs</sup> of Provinces Citties Townes Seas Rivers of <sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-007" href="#sec-07-fn-007" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-007" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Bridge &amp;c: &amp; to all other of what order or place soeu<sup>r</sup> haueing Comaund power Magistracie or Iurisdicc̃on health. Such as take vpon them greate matters aboue the vulgar vse and Custome verie proffitable to humane Societie deserue not onelie prayse and favo<sup>r</sup> of <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-325" data-page="325" data-folio="233" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n324_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 233">[p. 233]</button> all men, but alsoe aide and helpe asmuch as may be lawfullie &amp; convenientlie yealded. In the w<sup>ch</sup> kinde scarce any can be [        ]<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-009" href="#sec-07-fn-009" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-009" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> proffitable &amp; worthy then of them, the w<sup>ch</sup> by long Peregrinac̃ons greate [        ] danger of life, veiweing most remote &amp; vnknowne Nac̃ons &amp; kingdomes [        ] Sea doe seeke eu<sup>r</sup>y wheare all oportunities of Comodities, &amp; doe [        ]allie a kinde of Societie and Comunicac̃on of Comodities among all [        ]clude doe Contract the vniu<sup>r</sup>sitie of Mankinde &amp; the whole world [        ]e Cittie &amp; Communion. And forasmuch as c<sup>r</sup>teine o<sup>r</sup> subiectę [        ] this selfe same minde &amp; intencon take vpon them a Iourney [        ] that w<sup>th</sup>out the iniury or trouble of any man they may [        ]d Comodious meanes to o<sup>r</sup> subiectę of Interchandge of [        ] people &amp; of m<sup>r</sup>chandizeing thingę it be most iust and [        ] anitie that vnto the selfe same m<sup>r</sup>chauntę &amp; vnto their ffactor<sup>s</sup> [        ] and comeing by Sea and land be granted vnto them of all [        ]de, Yet that they may mor safelie and redilie proceede in their [        ]hey may haue the inclinac̃ons of all men of what order &amp; place [        ] the shall come more redy to graunte them that libertie, &amp; to be fauorable [        ] <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-326" data-page="326" data-folio="234" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n325_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 234">[p. 234]</button> w<sup>ch</sup> by the lawe of humanitie as is said before ought to be granted of [          ] wish the same to be obtenied by o<sup>r</sup> Intercession &amp; for that cause wee [          ]nient to giue theis o<sup>r</sup> łres of safe Conduct &amp; Comendačon to c<sup>r</sup>teine [          ]hauntę of o<sup>rs</sup> and namelie to Iohn Iourdan and Phillipp Glascocke by [          ]ie much intreate all &amp; singuler Kingę Princes Duke Marquesses Admiralls and superio<sup>rs</sup> of Provinces Townes Castles &amp; Portę &amp; all others of what degree and place soeu<sup>r</sup> haueing magistracy Iurisdicčon or power as may be agreeable to the order and dignitie of eu<sup>r</sup>y one, that you will geue leaue to the foresaid o<sup>r</sup> subiectę &amp; either of them, or to any other that shall bring theis o<sup>r</sup> łres vnto you by Land or sea to goe or to saile to goe forward or retourne &amp; as their occasion shall requier to remaine in their dominions &amp; that they will not geue or suffer any lett hindrance Iniurie or molestačon to be done vnto them, but that they will yeald vnto them such Offices of benevolence and humanitie as shall be convenient to be yealded to honest men and strangers vndertakeing soe longe and laborious a Iourney: handling their owne affaires &amp; behaueing themselues honestlie &amp; peaceablie. In the w<sup>ch</sup> kinde of Office or any other that may mutuallie be expected of vs we will be most redie to retourne the like favo<sup>r</sup> vnto all &amp; singuler yf occasion shall serue as shall be due to eu<sup>r</sup>y one according to their order place &amp; dignitie Giuen out of o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westm̄ this xxiii<sup>th</sup> of ffebruarie 1607.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><em>JAMES REX.</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Coppie of a lre sent to m<sup>r</sup> Alexander Sharpie Genrall.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-010" href="#sec-07-fn-010" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-010" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-011" href="#sec-07-fn-011" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-011" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup>&amp; of the order of Courte not to deale in any other manner but by way of marchantdize. 11 of March 1607</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER my hartie Comendačons &amp;c: According to order of a Courte of the Adventurers in this present 4<sup>th</sup> voyadge held the fift of this instant Theis are to advertice you that the Companie are pleased and soe doe thinke it fitt that you w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> Leiuetenante Generall m<sup>r</sup> Rowles, &amp; such as may happen to succeede you in this voyadge doe make espetiall accompt of <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-327" data-page="327" data-folio="235" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n326_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 235">[p. 235]</button> Ioseph Salbancke<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-012" href="#sec-07-fn-012" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-012" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> aswell in all yo<sup>r</sup> Councells as otherwise, &amp; the rather for that they doe esteeme him to be a man verie sufficient for any place wherein you shall vse him. And further the Companie thinke it meete that you doe carefullie reserue [and]<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-013" href="#sec-07-fn-013" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-013" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> keepe this lre amongst other yo<sup>r</sup> Comissions, that yo<sup>u</sup> and such as</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><blockquote><p>“Sharpie Gen<sup>rall</sup> signifieing vnto him that the Companie are pleased, and thinke yt “fitt, that he w<sup>th</sup> the Leiuetenante Gen<sup>rall</sup> and such as shall succeede them doe “make espetiall vse of Ioseph Salbancke in their Councell, &amp; otherwyse vnlesse they “shall finde some iuste reason to the Contrarie. And further willinge him to keepe “and reserue this his lre amongst other his Comissions that he and such as shall “succeede him may take notice thereof, as there shall be occasion.” (<em>Court Minutes</em>, 5th March, 1607/8.) “Att this Courte his ma<sup>ties</sup> lres and Comissions w<sup>th</sup> his ma<sup>ties</sup> saffe Conduct, &amp; “their Coppies in English were now deliu<sup>red</sup> to m<sup>r</sup> Sharpie, w<sup>th</sup> expresse Admonicon “that they doe their best endevo<sup>rs</sup> for the performeing of this voyadge by way of honest “m<sup>r</sup>chandize, to w<sup>ch</sup> end &amp; purpose onelie we haue sett them forth &amp; furnished them “w<sup>th</sup>all thinges needfull &amp; necessarie to that effect, w<sup>th</sup> expresse order alsoe that they “doe abstaine from dealeing w<sup>th</sup> any person in beraeueing manner, nor in any other “then in freindlie sorte: vnlesse onelie w<sup>th</sup> such who shall seeke to provoke them “therevnto, by any hindrance to their quiett trade: by taking of any o<sup>r</sup> goodes &amp;c: “According to the Teno<sup>r</sup> of his ma<sup>tes</sup> Comission now by vs procured for them. Aswell “for the avoiedinge of all scandall to the Company as for performance of his ma<sup>tes</sup>: “said Comission, as they will awnsweare the Contrarie.” (<em>Ibid.</em>, 11th March, 1607/8.) <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-328" data-page="328" data-folio="236" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n327_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 236">[p. 236]</button> may [happen] to succeede yo<sup>u</sup> as aforesaid may take notice thereof from tyme to [tyme as there] shall be occasion. Wherew<sup>th</sup> I Comitt yo<sup>u</sup> all to the Tuition of [Almightie God who] send yo<sup>u</sup> a prosperous successe in all yo<sup>r</sup> affaires. London th[         ] [Thomas Smyth]</p></blockquote></div><h2 class="sec-h1">To o<sup>r</sup> loueing freind m<sup>r</sup> Willm Kealinge Gen<sup>r</sup>all in o<sup>r</sup> third voyadge to the East Indies, &amp; in his absence to such other of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors as shall be Resident at Bantam or ells where in the said East Indies.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-014" href="#sec-07-fn-014" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-014" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></h2><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Copie of this lre deliured to m<sup>r</sup> Sharpie Generall for his better vnderstandinge what is on their parte to be done therein.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Twoe copies sent one to m<sup>r</sup> Kealing &amp; an other to m<sup>r</sup> Hawkins wth this Superscripcon.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER o<sup>r</sup> hartie Comendañons &amp;c: Since yo<sup>r</sup> depture from o<sup>r</sup> Coaste in Aprill last we haue not heard from you the w<sup>ch</sup> we conceiue to happen by noe default in you, but onelie for that you haue not had any meanes to write vnto vs, &amp; we doubt not but ere this you are all in saffetie arriued to some of o<sup>r</sup> desiered Porte in the Indies accordinge to yo<sup>r</sup> Comission where we hope that by yo<sup>r</sup> good industrie and carriadge we shall finde sufficient &amp; proffitable trade, &amp; likewise haue those retournes w<sup>ch</sup> you shall make home in such Comodities as for their qualitie and goodnes will be more beneficiall vnto vs, then hitherto we haue had as before yo<sup>r</sup> depture hence, &amp; by by yo<sup>r</sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-329" data-page="329" data-folio="237" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n328_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 237">[p. 237]</button> instruccõns you haue beene sufficientlie informed, &amp; therefore we make noe question but you will doe yo<sup>r</sup> vtmost endevo<sup>rs</sup> therein as we put o<sup>r</sup> trust in you.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And resolving the Continuance of o<sup>r</sup> trade in those pte as we haue at yo<sup>r</sup> being heare ympted vnto you, we haue now prepared twoe shipps (viz) the Assencõn &amp; the Vnion of w<sup>ch</sup> shipps and men by Comission from o<sup>r</sup> kinge ma<sup>tie</sup>: m<sup>r</sup> Alexander Sharpie is o<sup>r</sup> Generall in the Assencõn &amp; m<sup>r</sup> Richard Rowles his Leiuetenante Gen<sup>r</sup>all in the Vnion by whome we haue now sent theis o<sup>r</sup> lres vnto you, and thoughe wee cannot but giue you to vnderstand that this voyadge is solie one ioincte stocke seu<sup>r</sup>all from any of the former voyadge, yett the same is sett forth by some alsoe interessed in the third voyadge, whoe are authorized &amp; allowed by the gen<sup>r</sup>all Companie to doe the same And are inclined &amp; authorized the next yeare to doe the like. Wherefore as the forenamed Generall and others now sent haue order for their pte to doe the best Assistaunce they cann, soe we wish you to shew them all kindnes and favo<sup>r</sup> you may, aswell in the direccõn of their trade &amp; saffetie as otherwise, yett soe as the Accompte be noe way mingled, nor the proceedinge of either, be any way preiudiceall thone to thother &amp; they Comeing to any place wherein you shall be Resident as loueing Brothers and servante of one Companie you are to instruct them by the best meanes you may howe you haue there proceeded w<sup>th</sup> the Superio<sup>rs</sup> of the place, &amp; what you haue done conc<sup>r</sup>ninge the setlinge of yo<sup>r</sup> trade, &amp; howe the same is likelie to stand. And alsoe of what els shall be fitt for the Comon good, that you w<sup>th</sup> them &amp; they w<sup>th</sup> you may soe agree in Concorde that all thinge may be soe done as both you and we may haue comforte of yo<sup>r</sup> proceedinge therein.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And forasmuch as that o<sup>r</sup> men the w<sup>ch</sup> you shall leaue in those pte may haue some occasion of longer staie theare before retourne of o<sup>r</sup> shipps from hence thither vnto them againe, then we at yo<sup>r</sup> being heare intended: we wish you to giue them expresse chardge soe to carie them selues that they may be esteemed good husbande for the Companie, rather increasers then diminishers of what stocke you shall leaue w<sup>th</sup> them. And it is expected that by their good industrie in buieing of Cashes <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-330" data-page="330" data-folio="238" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n329_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 238">[p. 238]</button> &amp; other ymploimentę they may easilie beare their chardges theare, w<sup>th</sup> ou<sup>r</sup>plus to the proffitt of the Companie w<sup>ch</sup> as is reported they bring to their owne priuate the w<sup>ch</sup> we cannot endure And well pvsing ou<sup>r</sup> o<sup>r</sup> łres by you sent vnto them doe vse their best endevor<sup>s</sup> to applie them selues diligentlie to pforme o<sup>r</sup> direcc̃ons therein mencioned, &amp; what els, by experience they may learne, that when o<sup>r</sup> shipps shall come thither both we &amp; the may finde them to haue beene both diligent industrious &amp; good and faithfull Stewardę to the Companie, &amp; for better pformance of o<sup>r</sup> said direcc̃ons, we haue now sent them a Coppie of o<sup>r</sup> foresaid łres by you m<sup>r</sup> Kealinge sent the last yeare. And further theis are to requier you that if the shipps of the Adventurers in the 4<sup>th</sup> voyadge shall happen to come where you shall be resident &amp; wante ladeing of their owne. That then &amp; in such case you haueing any goodę to send home you doe lade soe much of the best thereof as convenientlie they shall be able to take in, being it much better that the same be soe brought home then that it should lie theare dead: wherein you are to haue due care that the Accomptę of either pte be noe way mingled &amp; that yo<sup>r</sup> goodę sent doe come vnder a seu<sup>r</sup>all Marke, Consigned by Bills of ladinge to the vse of the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie of the Adventurers in the third voyadge, takeing the best order that you can for keepinge of the seu<sup>r</sup>all goodę of both voyadgę apte thone from thother &amp; that łres be writte what shall be remaineinge for each Accompt. And further you shall vnderstand That if by theis meanes you shall send home any of o<sup>r</sup> goodę by the said shipps of the Adventurers in the 4<sup>th</sup> voyadge &amp; for the same or for some other cause there shall be noe need or ymployment for soe many of o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> there w<sup>ch</sup> may happen to be left. Then we doe order that some of the most insufficientest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> there be brought home by the said shipps in the 4<sup>th</sup> voyadge if in the discrec̃on of the now Generall m<sup>r</sup> Sharpie &amp; the cheeffest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> there it shall be soe thought fitt. And alsoe if the Adventurers in the said 4<sup>th</sup> voyadge shall leaue any ffactor<sup>s</sup> in the Indies where you shall be resident, That the ffactor<sup>s</sup> in both the voyadgę doe remaine and liue together At the chardge equallie p rato accordinge to the quantitie of the goodę w<sup>ch</sup> shall be there left for each accompt And thus not doubting of yo<sup>r</sup> faithfull care <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-331" data-page="331" data-folio="239" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n330_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 239">[p. 239]</button> and diligence in the premisses we pray God to blesse you all London the 12<sup>th</sup> day of March 1607.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> loueing ffreinde the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> Comitties &amp; Generalitie of the Companie of m<sup>r</sup>chante trading into the East Indies.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">This <sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-016" href="#sec-07-fn-016" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-016" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> is the draught of the Bill of Aduenture Ph: Graeff urget (?) to haue.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">TO ALL<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-015" href="#sec-07-fn-015" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-015" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to whome this present writing shall come to be seene herd or red wee the gouerno<sup>r</sup> and Companie of Marchaunte of London trading into the Easte Indians send greeting in our Lord god everlasting Whereas wee the said Gouerno<sup>r</sup> and Companie of Marchaunte haue Entertayned Phillip de Grauæ<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-017" href="#sec-07-fn-017" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-017" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> of the precinct of S<sup>t</sup> Katherins neare the Tower of London Marrioner to goe as principall m<sup>r</sup> for o<sup>r</sup> vses in this p̄nte voyadge nowe by vs prepared towardes the saide Easte Indians Knowe yee therefore that wee the saide Gouernor and Companie of Marchaunte for the further allowance and recompence of the good service trauell and paines w<sup>ch</sup> hee the said Phillip de Grauæ intendeth to p̄forme in the said voyage doe by these p̄nte for o<sup>r</sup> selues and everie of vs and the Executors and adm̄strators of vs and everie of vs Covenante promise and graunte to and w<sup>th</sup> the said Phillip de Grauæ his executors adm̄strators and assignes, To give pay and allowe vnto him the saide Phillip de Grauæ his executors adm̄strators or assignes oute of o<sup>r</sup> proper stockes aduentured in the said voyadge w<sup>th</sup>in six Monethes next after that the said shippes w<sup>ch</sup> is now bounde of the saide voyadge to the East Indians or any of them shall returne into the River of Thames neighe the port of London and <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-332" data-page="332" data-folio="240" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n331_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 240">[p. 240]</button> all the goode laide on land in London The benifitt proffitt and gaine that shall growe of ffive hundred and ffiftie poundes sterling (according to the rate of such an adventure in the said voyadge) oute of the gaine w<sup>ch</sup> god shall send by the returne of the said voyage, (The Charges of the voyadge &amp; of the goode returned therevppon groweing dew to be paied being ffirste deducted) In wittnes whereof wee haue herevnto Caused o<sup>r</sup> Common Seale to be put and deliuered this as o<sup>r</sup> deede yeeven the day of 1607 Annoq RR<sup>is</sup>: Iacobi Anglie &amp;c<sup>a</sup> quinto Et Scotie quadragesimo primo.</p></div><h2 class="sec-h1"><em>IN the name of God Amen the last of Februarie 1607.</em></h2><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">A Comission<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-018" href="#sec-07-fn-018" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-018" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> set downe by vs the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> Deputie and Comitties of the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London trading to the East Indies for better direcc̃on of o<sup>r</sup> loueing freinde the Generall Leuietenante &amp; other the Captaines m<sup>r</sup>chante Masters &amp; Mariners in this o<sup>r</sup> intended fourth voyadge to those pte w<sup>ch</sup> we humblie beseech god to blesse w<sup>th</sup> a happie Conclusion.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><span class="sc">I</span> 2 ships prepared &amp; m<sup>r</sup> Sharpie appointed Gen<sup>r</sup>all</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Inprimis</span> whereas we the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte aforesaid haue prepared w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> greate chardge &amp; trouble Twoe Shipps (viz) the Assenc̃on of 350 Tonns Phillipp de Graeffe m<sup>r</sup>, And the Vnion of 400 Tonns Griffin Morris<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-019" href="#sec-07-fn-019" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-019" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> m<sup>r</sup>, for a voyadge by Gode grace by them to be made to the East Indies, &amp; haue furnished them sufficientlie, &amp; in plentifull manner w<sup>th</sup> men Tackle victualls &amp; all other needfull prouisions fitt for such a voyadge: of a spetiall trust and Confidence w<sup>ch</sup> we haue in the integritie &amp; iust carrieinge of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind Alexander <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-333" data-page="333" data-folio="241" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n332_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 241">[p. 241]</button> Sharpie we doe appointe and authorize him Gen<sup>r</sup>all to goe in the Assenc̃on and to Comand o<sup>r</sup> said shipps &amp; all the men in them haueing procured him sufficient authoritie from o<sup>r</sup> souereigne Lord the kinge mat<sup>ie</sup>: for that purpose not doubtinge but that he will soe behaue himselfe as he may be both feared and loued &amp; that he will w<sup>th</sup> all his diligence and endevo<sup>r</sup> prosecute o<sup>r</sup> designes and labo<sup>r</sup> to bringe this o<sup>r</sup> intended voyadge to a happie end.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Leiuetenante Generall. 2</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item we appointe and ordeine o<sup>r</sup> loueing freind Richard Rowles to be Leiuetenante of o<sup>r</sup> said shipps &amp; men and to take his passadge in the Vnion, w<sup>ch</sup> Comanders ffactor<sup>s</sup> m<sup>rs</sup>: &amp; Mariners we trust will soe carrie them selues w<sup>th</sup> such due respect one to an other, and obedience to eu<sup>r</sup>y one of their Superio<sup>rs</sup> as loue &amp; kindnes be contynued on all side, &amp; all whollie applie their witte &amp; endevo<sup>rs</sup> for the due pformance of this voyadge, in the best manner they may for the good of the Companie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffor religious gou<sup>r</sup>nmt &amp; prayers dailie 3</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item and for that religious gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> &amp; exercise doth best binde men to pforme their duties it is principallie to be cared for that prayers be said morninge and eveninge in each shipp and the whole Companie called therevnto, w<sup>th</sup> diligent Eyes that none be wantinge that all ioinctlie may w<sup>th</sup> reuerence and humilitie pray vnto Almightie god to blesse and preserue them from all danger in this longe and tedious voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Against blasphemeinge &amp; vnlawfull games. 4</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that noe blasphemeing of god, swearing thefte dronkennes or otherlike disorders be vsed but that the same be sevearelie ponished, &amp; that noe diceinge or other vnlawfull games be admitted for that most comonlie the same is the begynninge of quarrellinge and many tymes murther a iust occasion of Gode wrath and vengeance from w<sup>ch</sup> the Lord deliu<sup>r</sup> vs. Therefore it shall be needfull that such orders be sett vp and published in each shipp and such punishm<sup>t</sup> appointed for such offence as may giue notice to eu<sup>r</sup>y man what he ought to avoied.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To abstaine from shedding liquor in the Ballast &amp; for keeping the shipps cleane 5</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that spetiall order be geven that noe liquo<sup>r</sup> be spilt in the ballast of the shipps, or filthines be left w<sup>th</sup>in bourd, w<sup>ch</sup> in heate breadeth noysome smells &amp; infecc̃on but that there be a diligent care to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-334" data-page="334" data-folio="242" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n333_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 242">[p. 242]</button> keepe the ou<sup>r</sup>lopps &amp; other places of the shipps cleane and sweete w<sup>ch</sup> is a notable preservac̃on of health, wherein the Dutchmen doe farr exceede vs in cleanlines to their greate Comendac̃ons &amp; disgrace to o<sup>r</sup> People.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffor keepinge Iournalls. 6</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that Continuall and true Iournalls be kept of eu<sup>r</sup>y daies Course &amp; Nauigac̃on during the whole voyadge w<sup>th</sup> a true relac̃on of eu<sup>r</sup>y thinge, that passeth &amp; this not onelie to be done by the seu<sup>r</sup>all Captaines, m<sup>rs</sup>, Pilatte &amp; m<sup>rs</sup> mate but alsoe by the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte and Pursars, &amp; that some of the principalls in each shipp may Conferr together at convenient tymes, when the Generall shall call them, therevnto, to thend that if any haue forgotten what an other obserued, the same may be added soe as a pfect discourse may be sett downe, to be presented to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie when god shall grante them a saffe retourne, to be kept for better direcc̃on of posteritie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffor keepinge Companie at Sea. 7</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that the Generall &amp; m<sup>r</sup> of his shipp doe sett downe &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup> such instrucc̃ons to the Comanders of thother shipp that they may keepe Companie together for the better releife and Comforte one of an other, And if separac̃on of them should happen by Constrainte of weather or Tempest that they should agree vpon a course before hand, &amp; of a place whither to applie their selues to finde each other.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To settle the seu<sup>r</sup>all provisions in each mans chardge after they shall come to Sea &amp; to deliu<sup>r</sup> nothinge out w<sup>th</sup>out warrant for w<sup>ch</sup> they are to be awnswerable vpon their wages. 8</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that the Gen<sup>r</sup>all Leiertenante &amp; m<sup>rs</sup> of the shipps take due notice by Inventory from eu<sup>r</sup>y Officer of the shipps before they depte out of the Thames of the seu<sup>r</sup>all provisions deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to each shipp &amp; soe sone as they shall come to Sea &amp; not before to settle the same in the chardge of the Officers appointed for that purpose in either shipp vntill w<sup>ch</sup> tyme all stores are to remaine vnder locke and key and that the same be entred in the Pursars Booke vnder written by the said Officers, to thend that such thinge as are provided for their needfull supplies may not be ymbecilled, &amp; that nothinge thereof of any value be ymployed for any vse whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>th</sup>out a warrante vnder the hand of the Generall directed to the Officer that hath such chardge, w<sup>ch</sup> warrante shall serue for their dischardge in their Accomptę, otherwise to be awnswearable for <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-335" data-page="335" data-folio="243" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n334_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 243">[p. 243]</button> the same w<sup>th</sup> their wage, &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y such warrant to be registred in the Pursars Booke the day of the monneth &amp; the occasion &amp; this vpon forfecture of soe much out of the Pursars wage, this order to be obserued alsoe in each of the shipps by the Comanders m<sup>rs</sup> Pursars &amp; Officers as aforesaid, that at the retourne of the said shipps a true Accompt may be yealded howe and in what mann<sup>r</sup> the same haue beene vsed &amp; ymployed.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Not to straggle 9</aside></div><p class="prose-p">And whereas the stragling of yo<sup>r</sup> men in any place where they shall arriue or come may proue not onelie the losse of such as shall soe straggle although they shall be Armed, but alsoe thereby much hurte may happen to the Residue of o<sup>r</sup> People &amp; generallie to the whole Companie. Yt is ordered that you doe not ꝑmitt any man in any place where you shall come (vpon what ground soeu<sup>r</sup>) to straggle or goe from the Residue of yo<sup>r</sup> Companies vnlesse they be by you first warranted therevnto, &amp; that vpon iust occasion, vpon paine of seveare ponishment to be inflicted of such as shall doe the Contrarie still doubtinge the worst and standinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> guarde night and day in eu<sup>r</sup>y place where you shall come w<sup>th</sup>out yealdinge any trust to those People.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">what is to be obserued at wateringe 10.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item we Comend vnto you the Generall principallie &amp; to the rest of the Comanders and m<sup>rs</sup>: an espetiall care that at eu<sup>r</sup>y place where you shall water and refresh yo<sup>r</sup> men you call the Companies together geueing them seveare warninge to behaue them selues peaceablie and Ciuillie towarde the People of that place the better to pcure their freindshipp towarde the supplie of yo<sup>r</sup> wante, &amp; the like in eu<sup>r</sup>y place wheare you come, least the losse of yo<sup>r</sup> liues &amp; ou<sup>r</sup>throwe of o<sup>r</sup> voyadge pay for yo<sup>r</sup> disorders, beside an vtter discreditt to o<sup>r</sup> Nacõn, and that yo<sup>r</sup> men doe vse a discreete meane in eatinge of fruite and fresh victualls least the surfett &amp; fall into diseases, whereof we haue had too much experience. And the better to refraine vnrulie appetite we hould it Convenient that the Generall appointe some of the discreetest in the Companies to procure &amp; prouide in such places what you wante, and the same being prouided and brought to the Generall, by him to be repted<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-020" href="#sec-07-fn-020" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-020" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to each Companie what may be needfull, and that none presume to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-336" data-page="336" data-folio="244" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n335_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 244">[p. 244]</button> doe the Contrarie w<sup>th</sup>out seueare ponishm<sup>t</sup>, And that a spetiall care be had to releeue the sicke w<sup>th</sup> such fresh meatę &amp; other Comfortable thingę wherew<sup>th</sup> we haue furnished each shipp for that purpose, &amp; not otherwyse to be spent in Royott and bankettinge, &amp; soe the sicke pꝛishe for wante of thingę needfull: Yet o<sup>r</sup> expresse order is that yo<sup>u</sup> neu<sup>r</sup> putt into any Harbor w<sup>th</sup>out greate occasion, &amp; then vpon c<sup>r</sup>teine knowledge of the Securitie of the place and vigilent care night and day to prevent Dangers takeing espetiall notice from place to place of the Course of the Monsomes for yo<sup>r</sup> better direcõns if you shall haue occasion to goe to diu<sup>r</sup>s places.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To water at St: Lawrence, &amp; sett vp their Pinnasse &amp; followe S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster’s direccons 11.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item for the fittest place of refreshinge we wish you to shape yo<sup>r</sup> Course to the Porte of S<sup>t</sup> Augustine in the Iland of S<sup>t</sup> Lawrence, where you shall finde good Harbor &amp; plentie of victualls, in w<sup>ch</sup> place it will not be fitt to make longe aboade but make hast and endevo<sup>r</sup> to be vpon the Coaste of Melinde in the monneth of Iulie at the ffurthest, but for yo<sup>r</sup> better direcõn therein we referr you to the instruccõns of S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-021" href="#sec-07-fn-021" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-021" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> now deliu<sup>r</sup>ed you herew<sup>th</sup>, whoe can best direct you for yo<sup>r</sup> course vpon that Coaste by his owne experience, except yo<sup>u</sup> in yo<sup>r</sup> discrecõns finde otherwyse more fitt: still standinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> guarde in eu<sup>r</sup>y place w<sup>th</sup>out yealding any trust to those People. At w<sup>ch</sup> place alsoe we hould it fitt, &amp; tyme will then best serue to that effect, that you settogether and make readie yo<sup>r</sup> Pinnace the w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> carrie w<sup>th</sup> you, &amp; alsoe you may heare informe yo<sup>r</sup> selues if o<sup>r</sup> shipps &amp; men sent the last yeare haue beene theare, when, &amp; w<sup>ch</sup> way they tooke &amp;c: for better direcõn of the Busines you are now sent aboute as occasion shall serue.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Beware of the fflattes of the coast of Melinde, &amp; what they are to buie &amp; obserue. 12</aside></div><p class="prose-p">ITEM after yo<sup>r</sup> depture from the Porte of S<sup>t</sup> Augustine towardę the Coast of Melynde beware of the fflattę of Iudea<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-022" href="#sec-07-fn-022" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-022" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> w<sup>ch</sup> lie betweene the Iland of S<sup>t</sup> Lawrence &amp; the firme land: vpon w<sup>ch</sup> fflattę it is said that the Currantę doe sett. There is alsoe an Iland vpon the Coaste of Melinde verie fitt for refreshinge yo<sup>r</sup> people as we are informed &amp; whereof the Hollenders had experience in their last voyadge that way: The w<sup>ch</sup> Iland is <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-337" data-page="337" data-folio="245" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n336_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 245">[p. 245]</button> called Zinzabar where if you finde it needfull &amp; haue tyme for that purpose you may theare water and refresh yo<sup>r</sup> Companies, els not. And alsoe it shall not be amisse as you saile alonge w<sup>th</sup>out staye in any place or goeinge a shoare to buie some Oliphauntę Teeth w<sup>ch</sup> are bought at Mozambique &amp; on the Coaste of Melinde in greate abondance, and carried into the Indies. That Commoditie is exceedinglie requested in Cambaia alsoe stoare of Ambergreece is likewise to be had in the Coaste of Melinde and at S<sup>t</sup> Lawrence Iland: But goe not either to Mozambique nor to the Iland of Pemba in any hand but leaue it on yo<sup>r</sup> larbo<sup>r</sup>side.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To make diligent inquirie of all thinges needfull 13</aside></div><p class="prose-p">It is further ordered that all the way alonge as you saile, or wheare you shall come you doe yo<sup>r</sup> best endevo<sup>r</sup> to vnderstand what good saffe Harbo<sup>rs</sup> are in each place to be had for vs to trust vnto, for maintenance of a Trade in those pte in saffetie from danger of Portugalls or others: vnder whome in each place the People are in subiection: &amp; of what nature they are: what havens they be, of what condicõn &amp; vnder whose Comand, together w<sup>th</sup> the dangers &amp; depthes of waters in each place for passadge what trade may be thear had, the sorte prices and goodnes of Comodities, as alsoe what sorte of o<sup>rs</sup> are there best requested as likewyse by what speedie meanes you may send or bringe vs tres ou<sup>r</sup>land of what shall be fitt for vs to haue knowledge of by way of Turkie or otherwise, and for yo<sup>r</sup> better saffetie in yo<sup>r</sup> passadge therein (if need be) we haue ꝑcured you his ma<sup>ties</sup>: saffe Conduct<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-023" href="#sec-07-fn-023" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-023" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> the w<sup>ch</sup> we haue geven to you the Generall to be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to such as shall be vsed in this respect, endevoring as Convenientlie you may w<sup>th</sup>out any ympedim<sup>t</sup> to yo<sup>r</sup> voyadge findinge thingę proffitable, to vse the best meanes to drawe the people vnto you in their Iunckę or Boatę w<sup>th</sup> some of their m<sup>r</sup>chandize for w<sup>ch</sup> you may assay to barter some of o<sup>r</sup> owne, or sell and buie as you shall see occasion: As alsoe endevoringe to doe &amp; vnderstand what ells shall be for the Comon good of the Companie, &amp; for future experience.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Some order to be sett downe touching dead mens goodes 14.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">ITEM it is expresslie ordered that by a Generall Consent, of <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-338" data-page="338" data-folio="246" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n337_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 246">[p. 246]</button> you the Generall Captaine ffactor<sup>rs</sup> Masters &amp; Mariners some good order be by you taken &amp; sett downe in writeinge soe soone as you come to Sea, conc<sup>r</sup>ninge the disposeing of dead mens goode in the voyadge, by sale at the Maste, or otherwise for buieinge sellinge borrowinge &amp; lendinge one an other, &amp; ordering what sallarie shall be allowed the Pursars vpon eu<sup>r</sup>y pound for keepinge Accompt thereof to avoied Controu<sup>r</sup>sie and Question hereafter, &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y Mariner and other of each shipp to sett their hand or marke vnto the same order. But if any ffactor shall happen to die his goode, Booke, Accompte, &amp; other thinge are to be by order of the Generall and Leiuetenant Generall saffelie reserued &amp; brought home for England.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffor better p<sup>r</sup>servacon of health by washing Caske 15.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffor inquirie for the Suzan. 16.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item for better preseruac̃on of health o<sup>r</sup> order is that from tyme to tyme as the water Caske shall be emptied of fresh water it be forthw<sup>th</sup> againe filled w<sup>th</sup> salt water for preven c̃on of leakeinge and Mustines Item o<sup>r</sup> direc̃con is that you make diligent inquirie at S<sup>t</sup> Lawrence &amp; Zinzabar, &amp; all other places in yo<sup>r</sup> passadge and retourne of Tydinge of the Suzan:<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-024" href="#sec-07-fn-024" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-024" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> or if any English shipp hath beene of late w<sup>th</sup>in theis two yeares either cast away or taken, or putt in for reliefe in any of those pte &amp; receiueing any knowledge of her to vse the best meanes you may for recou<sup>r</sup>y of her, her men, and goode, Soe as it may be noe way p<sup>r</sup>iudicious to yo<sup>r</sup> proceedinge in yo<sup>r</sup> voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To go thence for Zocatora &amp; soe to Aden &amp; Moccha comeinge in tyme els not &amp; what they are to do theare 17.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item haueing refreshed yo<sup>r</sup> People w<sup>th</sup>all speede you are to shape yo<sup>r</sup> course fo<sup>r</sup> Zocotora nere the mouth of the Red Sea, where is alsoe good refreshinge &amp; (as is said) the best Alloes Sacatrina in the world &amp; likewise stoare of Amber greece, &amp; other thinge whereof we pray you to take notice, &amp; buie thereof, what you shall see convenient but still in all yo<sup>r</sup> m<sup>r</sup>chandizeing, be carefull to putt of yo<sup>r</sup> English Comodities in Trucke. In this Iland we doubt not but you may be furnished both w<sup>th</sup> Pilotte for Aden: Or if the same be not fitt for trade by reason as it is reported vnto vs the same is a Garrison Towne rather of Souldiers <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-339" data-page="339" data-folio="247" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n338_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 247">[p. 247]</button> then Marchaunte.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-025" href="#sec-07-fn-025" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-025" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Then you are to goe for Moccha a Towne 10 miles from Aden said to be a place of speciall trade right ouer against w<sup>ch</sup> Towne there is an Iland called Kedgee where is alsoe reported to be good Harbor and wateringe (if need shall soe requier) and vnderstandinge what trade there is to be had, what the People be and of what interteinm<sup>t</sup>: toward strangers, whereof informe yo<sup>r</sup> selues in the best manner you may, as alsoe if o<sup>r</sup> shipps and men sent thither the last yeare haue been at Aden, or <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-340" data-page="340" data-folio="248" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n339_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 248">[p. 248]</button> theare aboute, &amp; beinge theare what they haue done, and soe findinge of trade and interteinm<sup>t</sup> w<sup>th</sup>all convenient speede you are to direct yo<sup>r</sup> course for Aden or Moccha or some other place theareaboute w<sup>ch</sup> you may learne to be more fitter for trade and interteynm<sup>t</sup>, Soe alwaies Provided you come thither about the fine of Iulie, or in such tyme (if need be) as you may goe thence w<sup>th</sup> that Monsone for Surate or Bantam, the w<sup>ch</sup> we conceiue you may verie well pforme yf (you findinge not thinge there to yo<sup>r</sup> likeinge,) you make noe staye theare but w<sup>th</sup>all speede depte thence for Surate by the 20th of September at the furthest the w<sup>ch</sup> you are in any case to doe ells you shall loose the oportunitie of that Monsone, where vnto you are both in goeing and retourninge to haue an espetiall care. And beinge arriued neare the place, come to an Anco<sup>r</sup> but take heed in any Case you come not w<sup>th</sup>in their danger, still expecting and feareing evill though there be noe cause.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And comeing thither first you are to vse meanes (if any of o<sup>r</sup> People be theare[])to deliu<sup>r</sup> o<sup>r</sup> lres<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-026" href="#sec-07-fn-026" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-026" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> by you sent accordinge to the direccon to m<sup>r</sup> Willm Hawkins or to the cheefe of o<sup>r</sup> men w<sup>ch</sup> shall be theare lefte, &amp; therevpon to Conferr together w<sup>th</sup> them and vnderstand what they haue done aboute the deliu<sup>r</sup>y of o<sup>r</sup> kinge ma<sup>te</sup> lres sent by them to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of that place w<sup>th</sup> a Present as alsoe for their quiett trade &amp;c: That you may the better pceede w<sup>th</sup> good aduice to doe accordinglie, aswell for deliu<sup>r</sup>y of his ma<sup>ties</sup> lres now sent by you to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of Aden or Moccha w<sup>th</sup> some Convenient present as otherwise as theare shall be occasion, and w<sup>th</sup>all you are to signifie vnto such of o<sup>r</sup> men as you shall finde there that this fowerthe voyadge is solie one ioincte stocke seuerall from the former Voyadge yet sett out by some of the same Companie authorized and allowed therevnto by the rest accordinge to the Teno<sup>r</sup> of the same lre now by vs sent vnto them, &amp; accordinglie throughout all the whole voyadge you are to keepe Accompt of the same as a ioincte stocke of it selfe, sett out by vs seu<sup>r</sup>allie from all the former voyadge for those pte. And further if thinge soe stand there as it shall be needfull, you may make it knowne to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of the place as we haue form<sup>r</sup>lie geven order to o<sup>r</sup> men to doe (that is) that you are English m<sup>r</sup>chaunte &amp; haue <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-341" data-page="341" data-folio="249" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n340_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 249">[p. 249]</button> brought tres vnto him from o<sup>r</sup> kinge ma<sup>tie</sup>: seekeinge onlie peaceable trade, aswell for saile of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities as for buieinge of theirs. And therew<sup>th</sup>all to deliu<sup>r</sup> the same tres w<sup>th</sup> such present as you shall thinke fitt to the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> w<sup>th</sup> whome (as you shall see cause) you may treate for Priuiledge and freedome of trade for as many Yeares as you can, if it be not by o<sup>r</sup> men form<sup>r</sup>lie done or needfull to be enlardged declaringe that o<sup>r</sup> kinge is in League w<sup>th</sup> the Grandsignio<sup>r</sup> That o<sup>r</sup> People doe trade into Cairo, Aleppo, Damasko, Constantinople, &amp; in all other his Dominions freelie and that o<sup>r</sup> kinge hath now a lidger Imbaffado<sup>r</sup> in Constantinople, w<sup>ch</sup> loue of the Grand Signio<sup>r</sup> towarde o<sup>r</sup> Nacõn, hath ymbouldened vs to visitt that place w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> shipps and goode now desieringe by his favorable lycence to trade as Ciuill &amp; peaceable m<sup>r</sup>chante &amp; that you desier to settle a ffactorie theare, yf by his honorable favo<sup>r</sup> you may be pmitted, assureing him that by o<sup>r</sup> trade his Customes will be greatelie increased for that we shall endevo<sup>r</sup> to fetch that from thence w<sup>ch</sup> we now fetch from the Molloccos Bantam &amp; diu<sup>r</sup>s other places in the Indies. To w<sup>ch</sup> if he Yeald it shall be needfull that you craue such articles of Priuiledge from him in writeinge for o<sup>r</sup> Nacõn the better to incurradge vs to frequent that place. And for yo<sup>r</sup> better direccon we haue deliu<sup>r</sup>ed you the Coppie of Priuiledge procured for vs by S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster from the Kinge of Achen, addinge therevnto what other thinge shall be needfull. And further if you shall see cause you may make the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> acquainted w<sup>th</sup> what sorte of English Comodities you haue now brought thither, but not (yf it may be) w<sup>th</sup> any of yo<sup>r</sup> moneys vntill yo<sup>r</sup> Comodities be first sould, at w<sup>ch</sup> place we choose rather to be, then to make triall of any other if possiblie we may.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffor better sale of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities &amp; buieinge of theirs at Aden.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item arriueinge at Aden or Moccha &amp; findinge good hope of interteynement &amp; trade whereby either presentlie or in some shorte space by the retourne of their Iouncke from Cambaia you may vent o<sup>r</sup> English Comodities, &amp; finde fitt and sufficient ladeinge there for retournes, we wish you that in the buieinge thereof that you be verie carefull to make choise of that w<sup>ch</sup> is good and fitt for sale, &amp; in sale of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities that you stand vpon the value &amp; estimaçon thereof, Yet soe as you loose not the oportunitie of <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-342" data-page="342" data-folio="250" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n341_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 250">[p. 250]</button> the first m<sup>r</sup>kette &amp; reasonable offers least by often veiwinge them they fall to dislike, for Nouelties as you knowe drawe men to buie: and note that by the repaier of all sorte of People thither you shall finde meanes to sell what Comodities of o<sup>rs</sup> are in any place requested yf you putt yo<sup>r</sup> selues forward, &amp; by helpe of some Brokers you seeke to deale: But you must be carefull to vse all men w<sup>th</sup> kindnes and Ciuillitie, and see that yo<sup>r</sup> men doe the like in any wise, least disorders and scandalous behauio<sup>r</sup> cause you to be abhorred and evill intreated. At w<sup>ch</sup> place as we esteeme it to be the most saffest nearest and cheeffest place for trade we desier rather to be, then at any other, if we may there soe finde proffitable and sufficient trade in saffetie as aforesaid.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffor Adverticement ourland if need be &amp; by what meanes. 19.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item if you finde theare that any of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities or Colo<sup>rs</sup> will sell better then those we now send, or some of those more vendible then the rest we desier to be adverticed ourland by some Moores that goe to Argier Aleppo or Alexandria and direct yo<sup>r</sup> łres to Richard Allen Consull at Argier to be sent hither w<sup>th</sup> speede, or to Aleppo to m<sup>r</sup> Paule Pyndar<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-027" href="#sec-07-fn-027" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-027" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Consull or to <sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-028" href="#sec-07-fn-028" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-028" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup>at Alexandria or any other English men that be there obserueing the note of a Caracter w<sup>ch</sup> we deliu<sup>r</sup> you herew<sup>th</sup> for better secrecie And not to be backward or slacke by eu<sup>r</sup>y Conveyaunce to write least some łres miscarrieinge others may come to hand.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Haueing procured yor lading at Aden to retourne for England. 20</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">noe ffactors names put into theis spaces left.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">And haueinge procured the dispatch of o<sup>r</sup> shipps w<sup>th</sup> their full ladeinge our direccon is that you retourne for England w<sup>th</sup> the next fitt winde and weather leaueing there for facto<sup>rs</sup> if you thinke needfull<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-028-2" href="#sec-07-fn-028" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-028" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> and such others as you thinke meete w<sup>th</sup> some convenient allowance &amp; likewise w<sup>th</sup> order to ymploye their endevo<sup>rs</sup> for findinge out of further trade aswell for all sorts of Cloth and Kersies and Colo<sup>rs</sup> best requested, as all other of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities and for the buieing of all those Countrie wares obserueing still good and c<sup>r</sup>teine note of all sorte of Comodities brought hither from out of the East Indies: But if <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-343" data-page="343" data-folio="251" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n342_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 251">[p. 251]</button> after yo<sup>r</sup> arriuall to Aden or Moccha you shall happen not to finde sufficient meanes theare to relade home both yo<sup>r</sup> shipps (as at first you expected) but onelie one of them, nor any hope of further trade there likelie in any good tyme to be made, then o<sup>r</sup> will is you should w<sup>th</sup> all expedic̃on send home one of yo<sup>r</sup> shipps soe laden for</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">England vnder the Comand of o<sup>r</sup> Leiuetenante Gen<sup>r</sup>all &amp; thother to goe forward for Cambaia or Bantam as the tyme shall be fitt therevnto, &amp; there shall be convenient stocke to doe the same. Betweene w<sup>ch</sup> place at the mouth of the Red Sea &amp; Cambaia &amp; in the River Syndus as we are informed there is noe doubt of Ample and beneficiall trade in saffetie yf you make diligent search and inquirie for the same wherein we pray you to haue espetiall care. And for yo<sup>r</sup> better pceedinge therein w<sup>th</sup> any Prince or Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> you may finde vnto whome you haue not łres alreadie directed vnto them from o<sup>r</sup> kinge ma<sup>tie</sup>: we haue pcured you łres from his ma<sup>tie</sup>: w<sup>th</sup> gen<sup>r</sup>all termes in the inside &amp; w<sup>th</sup> Blancke on the outside to be by you directed to any such Prince or Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> if you shall happen to haue occasion: But before yo<sup>r</sup> depture one from annother the Leiuetenante Generall is to call vnto you the Generall for the duplicate of his ma<sup>ties</sup>: Comission vnder the broad Seale for better Gou<sup>r</sup>nment of his men homewarde.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Comeing not to Zocotora in due tyme to goe for Aden, to leaue Aden, &amp; goe directlie for Cambaia &amp; there to followe this &amp; the next ensueing orders.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">But yf it shall soe happen you come not to Zocotora in such due tyme as you may convenientlie goe to Aden &amp; haue that Monsone to bringe you into the Indies (if need be) doe not then at any hand goe to Aden or Moccha but saile directlie towarde Surate the entrance of Cambaia, where if you come aboute the middle of September you shall finde it Somer and faier weather soe as you may saffelie ride in the open Roade. But if the same place shall not be found fitt for trade according to o<sup>r</sup> expectac̃on by reason of some dangers that may happen vnto you by the Portugalls of Diu and Demon<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-029" href="#sec-07-fn-029" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-029" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> whoe as is said haue the Comand of those Seas &amp; receiue tribute of the People of those place, Then you are to saile for Lawrie<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-030" href="#sec-07-fn-030" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-030" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> in the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-344" data-page="344" data-folio="252" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n343_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 252">[p. 252]</button> Bay of the River <em>Syndus</em>. aboute 2 daies saileing from <em>Surate</em>, where, as we are informed is good Harbor in saffetie w<sup>th</sup>in 2 miles of w<sup>ch</sup> Towne vp in that Bay there is a greate Cittie as bigg as London called Negertuttie,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-031" href="#sec-07-fn-031" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-031" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> where is plentie of Indico Callicoes &amp; diu<sup>r</sup>s other m<sup>r</sup>chandizes fitt for England the people whereof are free, onelie gou<sup>r</sup>ned by the kinge of <sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-032" href="#sec-07-fn-032" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-032" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Arcabar whoe recideth at Agree some fortie English Miles from Negertuttie, at w<sup>ch</sup> place the Portugalls haue nothinge to doe, but onelie come thither for m<sup>r</sup>chandize, as other strangers, where it is conceyued if you behaue yo<sup>r</sup> selues well and gentlie towarde those people you will finde ample and proffitable trade aswell of their Comodities as for vent of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities theare, where it is reported Clothes of all sorte is in great request. At w<sup>ch</sup> place or places if vpon diligent inquirie you shall finde a c<sup>r</sup>teine and saffe expectac̃on of trade &amp; that o<sup>r</sup> shipps may goe into saffe Harbor<sup>s</sup> free from danger of the Portugalls or any other: Then you are to staye in the open Roade neare the Barr of Cambaia or at Lawrie &amp; by some of the People w<sup>ch</sup> come out eu<sup>r</sup>y Tyde from thence, or otherwise we wish you to doe yo<sup>r</sup> best endevo<sup>rs</sup> to vnderstand yf o<sup>r</sup> shipps and men sent for those pte the last yeare, haue beene there, &amp; being there what they haue done, &amp; soe findinge hope of saffetie &amp; interteinment to send word to some of o<sup>r</sup> men (yf any shall be there) that you are arriued wishing to haue Conference w<sup>th</sup> them before you make yo<sup>r</sup> comeing knowne to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of that place aswell conc<sup>r</sup>ninge what they haue done in the deliu<sup>r</sup>y of o<sup>r</sup> kinge ma<sup>ties</sup>: łres by them sent to the kinge of Cambaia &amp; Present as otherwise of what hope of interteinm<sup>t</sup> &amp; quiett trade may be there expected that therevpon you may proceed w<sup>th</sup> good advice to doe accordinglie <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-345" data-page="345" data-folio="253" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n344_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 253">[p. 253]</button> for deliu<sup>r</sup>y of o<sup>r</sup> kinge ma<sup>ties</sup>: łres &amp; Present now alsoe by you sent to the kinge of Cambaia as for the obtayneinge of quiett trade &amp; otherwise as there shall be occasion. And comeinge thither &amp; findinge some of o<sup>r</sup> men theare you are to deliu<sup>r</sup> o<sup>r</sup> łres now sent vnto them accordinge to the direcčõn, &amp; to signifie vnto them that this voyadge is solie one ioinct stocke seu<sup>r</sup>all from the former voyadge, and yett sett out by some of the same Company authorized and allowed therevnto by the rest, accordinge to the Teno<sup>r</sup> of the same łre from vs vnto them,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-033" href="#sec-07-fn-033" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-033" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> &amp; accordingelie through out all the whole voyadge you are to keepe Accompt of the same as of a ioincte stocke of it selfe by vs sett out seu<sup>r</sup>all from all the form<sup>r</sup> voyadge as is before specified in the 17 Article.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">22</aside></div><p class="prose-p">But if none of o<sup>r</sup> men be theare, &amp; notw<sup>th</sup>standing there be vpon informačõn good hope of quiett trade, &amp; kinde interteinem<sup>t</sup> in saffetie. Then at your arriuall as aforesaid you are neu<sup>r</sup>thelesse to send word to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> or Comanders there that there is a Messenger arriued w<sup>th</sup> łres from the kinge ma<sup>tie</sup>: of England vnto their kinge praying the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> to send them a boate w<sup>th</sup> saffe Conveyaunce. And if any come to you from the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> Then we thinke it fitt that you the Generall doe repaier thither takeing w<sup>th</sup> you his ma<sup>ties</sup>: łres &amp; Presente &amp; such other provisions as you haue for that purpose, &amp; in yo<sup>r</sup> Companie take w<sup>th</sup> you twoe of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> Reaue<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-033-2" href="#sec-07-fn-033" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-033" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> and Iourdan &amp; yo<sup>r</sup> owne servant whoe are barrelled to that effect or whome els you shall finde most meete for that purpose, &amp; comeinge to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of Surate or Lawrie present him w<sup>th</sup> some guifte what you shall thinke meete, &amp; desier them to assist you w<sup>th</sup> meanes and guide to their kinge that you may deliu<sup>r</sup> his ma<sup>te</sup> said łres &amp; present vnto that kinge craue a speedie awnsweare and his ma<sup>ties</sup>: resolučõn if he will be pleased or not to grante you free and saffe trade in his Countries and Harbo<sup>rs</sup> accordinge to the Teno<sup>r</sup> of his ma<sup>te</sup>: said łre w<sup>th</sup> sufficient defence against those that shall practise to ympeach or hinder the same, or annoy or hurte any of the shipps Bodies or goode of <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-346" data-page="346" data-folio="254" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n345_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 254">[p. 254]</button> any of o<sup>r</sup> Nac̃on. And the better to induce the kinge to grante vs Priviledge and free trade signifie that we will be readie to bringe him Armo<sup>r</sup> and other munic̃on from tyme to tyme in o<sup>r</sup> seu<sup>r</sup>all voyadge for the better inablinge him against his Enimyes beside the increase of his Customes w<sup>ch</sup> will be greater for that thereby we shall fetch that from thence w<sup>ch</sup> we now fetch from the Molloccos Bantam and diu<sup>rs</sup> other places in the Indies, &amp; that you seeke onelie peaceable trade aswell for buieinge of their Comodities as for sale of yo<sup>r</sup> owne, w<sup>ch</sup> if he promyse to doe, then desier to haue Comissioners appointed to Consider w<sup>th</sup> you of Articles in writeinge for setlinge of a ffactorie (yf need be) &amp; for Priuiledge of Trade for as many yeares as you can procure to be granted to o<sup>r</sup> Nac̃on the better to incourradge vs to frequent his kingdomes yf the same be not form<sup>r</sup>lie ꝑcured by o<sup>r</sup> men not<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-035" href="#sec-07-fn-035" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-035" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> needfull further to be inlardged. And for yo<sup>r</sup> better direcc̃on therein, we haue deliu<sup>r</sup>ed you the foresaid Coppie of the Priviledge form<sup>r</sup>lie procured for vs by S<sup>r</sup>: Iames Lancaster from the kinge of Achen,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-036" href="#sec-07-fn-036" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-036" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> addinge thervnto what other thinge shall be needfull: w<sup>ch</sup> Priviledge beinge obtayned and signed by the kinge, haueinge taken yo<sup>r</sup> leaue in Comelie manner, and receiued such Comoonde-m<sup>te</sup> in writeinge directed to his Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> and Officers of his Townes and Porte, as you can pcure for yo<sup>r</sup> defence against all disturbers of you in yo<sup>r</sup> peaceable trade. And for their Assistance of you &amp; kinde interteinm<sup>t</sup> in the same, yo<sup>u</sup> are w<sup>th</sup>all speede to make yo<sup>r</sup> retourne to Surate or to Lawrie aforemencioned: And haueing deliu<sup>r</sup>ed that kinge Comandement to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Officers there craue their Assistance w<sup>th</sup> Pilotte &amp; saffe Conducc̃on of yo<sup>r</sup> shipps &amp; therew<sup>th</sup> make all the expedic̃on you can to gett into the Harbo<sup>r</sup>: But first shewe to some trustie Brokers, paterns of such yo<sup>r</sup> Clothes kersies and other Comodities as are in o<sup>r</sup> shipps, whereof, as of such moneys we haue nowe sent you haue<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-037" href="#sec-07-fn-037" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-037" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> an Invoice, takeinge knowledge w<sup>ch</sup> sorte of those Comodities wilbe theare best sold &amp; accordinglie giue order for fetching of them a shoare in saffe manner. And further if you shall see cause you make the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> acquainted w<sup>th</sup> what sorte of English Comodities you haue now brought thither but not (yf <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-347" data-page="347" data-folio="255" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n346_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 255">[p. 255]</button> otherwise possiblie) w<sup>th</sup> any of yo<sup>r</sup> moneys vntill yo<sup>r</sup> Comodities be first sold.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffindinge vent att Cambaia for or commodities &amp; fitt retourne of theirs for England what the are to doe for sale &amp; buieinge thereof. 23.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">And findinge that you may vent o<sup>r</sup> English Comodities &amp; finde pffitable and sufficient ladinge there for retournes as Indico, Cotton yarne fine Callicoes Opium Gumlacre Beniamyn Muske Sivitt Amber Mirr, Olibinum, pretious stones Bezars &amp;c: we wish you that in the buieinge thereof you be verie carefull to make choise of that w<sup>ch</sup> is good &amp; fitt for sale, &amp; in sale of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities that you stand vpon the value and estimacõn thereof as in the 18<sup>th</sup> Article.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Haueing procured ye ladeinge of their ships at Cambaia to retourne home and leaue ffactors. 24</aside></div><p class="prose-p">And haueing procured the dispatch of o<sup>r</sup> shipps w<sup>th</sup> their full ladeinge at <em>Surate or Lawrie</em> or theareaboute of some proffitable Comodities as aforesaid the most ptę whereof to be Indico yo<sup>u</sup> are to retourne for England w<sup>th</sup> the next fitt wynde and weather leaueing there for ffactor<sup>rs</sup> (if you shall thinke needfull)<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-038" href="#sec-07-fn-038" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-038" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And such others as you shall appointe w<sup>th</sup> some convenient allowance, &amp; likewise w<sup>th</sup> order to ymploy all their endevo<sup>rs</sup> for findinge out of further trade aswell for all sorte of Clothes &amp; kersies as their Colo<sup>rs</sup> best requested as all other of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities, &amp; for the buieinge of those Countrie wares obserueinge still good &amp; c<sup>r</sup>teine note of all sorte of Comodities there to be sould as is form<sup>r</sup>lie mencioned in the 20<sup>th</sup> Article. Wheare alsoe before yo<sup>r</sup> retourne for England yo<sup>u</sup> are accordinge to yo<sup>r</sup> direcc̃on in the 17<sup>th</sup> article to take the best informac̃on you can of the state of Aden, as what Harbo<sup>rs</sup> and Pilotte to bringe men in, are theare to be had what Condic̃on the People are of what trade &amp; what interteinem<sup>t</sup> towarde strangers, what thinge will be there vendible &amp; saleable &amp;c: &amp; what els shall be needfull to be knowne in that respect, &amp; to acquainte vs therew<sup>th</sup> for o<sup>r</sup> better knowledge what to doe if hereafter at any tyme we shall thinke good to visitt that place.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">findinge ladinge but for one shipp to send her home by the Leiuetenante Genrall &amp; the duplicate of</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">his mates Comission &amp; send the other for Bantam haueinge but 2000<sup>li</sup> stocke to relade her thence. 25.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">But if after yo<sup>r</sup> arriuall to Surate or Lawrie or theareaboute you shall happen not to finde fitt and sufficient ladinge theare to relade home both yo<sup>r</sup> shipps as you first expected but onelie one of them, nor any <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-348" data-page="348" data-folio="256" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n347_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 256">[p. 256]</button> hope of ffurther trade there likelie in any reasonable tyme to be made, then o<sup>r</sup> will is if you haue but left of o<sup>r</sup> stocke sent w<sup>th</sup> you to the some of 2000<sup>li</sup> in moneys or in such Pinthadoes or Callicoes as you shall there puide for Bantam &amp; the Molloccos you shall w<sup>th</sup>all expedi<span class="sc">c</span>õn send home for England, one of yo<sup>r</sup> said shipps soe laden vnder the Comand of o<sup>r</sup> Leiuetenante Generall whoe before his depture from you is to call vnto you the Generall for the duplicate of his ma<sup>t<sup>e</sup></sup>: Comission vnder the Broade seale for the gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> of his men homewarde as aforesaid and the other shipp w<sup>th</sup>all expedi<span class="sc">c</span>õn to goe directlie thence for Bantam for better pformance whereof, you are soe to order yo<sup>r</sup> affaires at Surate, That you may be suer (if need be) you may haue the Monsone to bringe you thither.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">If all the stock nowe sent will fullie lade both the shipps then it is to be putt in both &amp; retourned for England. 26</aside></div><p class="prose-p">But if at yo<sup>r</sup> being at Surate or Lawrie or theareaboute you shall haue occasion by the Costlines of the Comodities you shall theare puide to ymploy all o<sup>r</sup> stocke of moneys &amp; Comodities you shall now carrie w<sup>th</sup> you, for such proffitable Comodities of those place as is before mencioned, &amp; the same will not be sufficient fullie to relade both the shipps home, Then o<sup>r</sup> will is you lade the same Comodities so bought in both the shipps equallie accordinge to their Bourthen &amp; retourne w<sup>th</sup> all expedi<span class="sc">c</span>õn directlie for England leaueing there some ffacto<sup>rs</sup> w<sup>th</sup> Convenient allowance yf you shall thinke meete as aforesaid.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">But findinge not trade at Cambaia to goe directlie for Bantam, &amp; what is there ordered to be done. 27</aside></div><p class="prose-p">But yf at Surate or Lawrie or theareaboute you be not kindlie dealt w<sup>th</sup>all nor cannott by any meanes finde interteinm<sup>t</sup> in saffetie or sufficient Comõditie to relade one or both of yo<sup>r</sup> shipps home. Then you are w<sup>th</sup> both the shipps and Pinnace to shape yo<sup>r</sup> course alonge the Coaste of Mallabar w<sup>th</sup> that Monsone for Bantam w<sup>th</sup> such Pinthadoes and Callicoes as you can gett for those place where you haueinge deliu<sup>r</sup>ed our tres accordinge to the direc<span class="sc">c</span>õn of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> at Bantam &amp; made them acquainted that what we now send is a ioincte stocke seu<sup>r</sup>allie of it selfe, accordinge to the Teno<sup>r</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> said tre as before is mencioned in the 12<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-039" href="#sec-07-fn-039" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-039" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> &amp; 17 Articles we referr you to Consider w<sup>th</sup> them theare <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-349" data-page="349" data-folio="257" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n348_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 257">[p. 257]</button> what is best to be done wherein vsinge such direcc̃ons for yo<sup>r</sup> reladeinge homewardę as you shall be then best advised: either w<sup>th</sup> Mace Nutmegge &amp; Cloues at the Mollocos, or w<sup>th</sup> the same and Pepp. Wherein alsoe you are to remember and foresee that in ladeing those Comodities you buie such as are drie &amp; not greene, &amp; that you cleanse them what you can that the ships be not stuffed w<sup>th</sup> such vnproffitable ware as is not worth the Custome, wherein we pray you to omitt noe tyme.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">See you haue yor weight &amp; the Bagges nombred &amp; mrked. 28</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item be verie carefull that you haue yo<sup>r</sup> due weight &amp; the same putt into Bagge nombred and m<sup>r</sup>ked &amp; sent abourd the shipps w<sup>th</sup> some of the ffactors or some other trustie p̄sons w<sup>th</sup> a note conteyneinge the p̄ticuler of eu<sup>r</sup>y Bagge vnder the nomber, the same note to be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to the Pursar whome we chardge to weigh eu<sup>r</sup>y Bagg at the takeing of it into the shipp, wherby he may see that the weight is accordinge to the note receiued, keepinge eu<sup>r</sup>y note &amp; entringe the Contente into his booke, whereby yf any error happen it may be found out w<sup>ch</sup> we wish you the Generall to examine eu<sup>r</sup>y night that there growe noe abuse.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffor sale of victuall &amp;
