Canvesse &amp;c: 29.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item forasmuch as in former voyadge soe now in this theare may be occasion for the benefitt of the Companie to make sale of some of yo<sup>r</sup> Victualls Canvesse or some other thinge belonging to yo<sup>r</sup> stoare, or otherwise whereof you shall happen to haue noe neede. Yt is therefore ordered that good care be had that the same be done not onlie for the proffitt of the Companie w<sup>th</sup>out any danger to the voyadge: But alsoe brought (as all other thinge) to the gen<sup>r</sup>all Accompt of the Companie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Pursars chardge for keepeing a true accompt of all thinges brought in &amp; caried out of the shipps 30.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item we chardge that the Pursar be continuallie abourd the shipp and take true notice of all thinge, whatsoeur are brought into the shipp or carried out, either by the Gen<sup>r</sup>all Captaines, m<sup>rs</sup>: Mariners the Pursar himselfe, or whosoeu<sup>r</sup> the same to be entred into his Booke vnder the name of the owner that eu<sup>r</sup>y one may haue his due at the retourne as the Pursar will awnsweare it w<sup>th</sup> the losse of his wage and we pray the Generall and Captaines to geue the Pursar strict order for p̄formance thereof.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-350"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="350" data-folio="258" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n349_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 258">[p. 258]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">One hatch onelie open at the ladinge of the shipps &amp; leaueing worke to lock it &amp;c: <strong>31</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that there be but one hatch open (yf it may be) at the ladinge of either of the shipps &amp; all the other hatches lockt vpp &amp; that the m<sup>r</sup> or one of his mate: &amp; the Pursar to stand by the open hatches when any goode are shott into hould, lockinge the same Vpp when they haue left worke, the key to be geven to the Generall or Capteine to be kept.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To buie what is good &amp; not to ourwett any of the Comodities <strong>32</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item to be carefull that nothinge be bought but what is especiall good, &amp; that you doe not ou<sup>r</sup>wett the Cloues when they be laden, for ou<sup>r</sup>wett Cloues bad Nutmegge &amp; Mace haue heretofore beene a greate hindrance vnto vs &amp; taught vs to advise you to be more carefull.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To admitt noe priuate [trade] to any in any of theis Comodities vpon penaltie to loose the same <strong>33.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item for that voyadges of this Condic̃on &amp; greate chardge cannot admitt any priuate trade o<sup>r</sup> will is that neither you the Generall nor any other vpon any pticular, or other Accompt, then the Generall and iointe stocke doe lade or deale w<sup>th</sup> any of those Comodities herevnder expressed Viz Pepp, Cloues, Nutmegge, Mace, Synamon, Gynger, Indico, Callicoes, Amber greece, Muske, Alloes, Beniamyn, and Gumlacre. And that yf any shall soe doe the same to be applied to the Accompt of the Companie as our propper goode it beinge noe way reasonable that we should paie them their wage w<sup>th</sup> meate and drinke, &amp; they lade those thinge in o<sup>r</sup> shipps w<sup>ch</sup> should defray o<sup>r</sup> chardge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To compare their weightes &amp; measures wth ors before you buie. <strong>34.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item in eu<sup>r</sup>y place where you come and trade Compare their weighte &amp; measures w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>rs</sup> before you buie, to thend you may knowe the better what to doe &amp; howe to make yo<sup>r</sup> reckoninge for w<sup>ch</sup> purpose we haue put sundrie Cheste w<sup>th</sup> weighte into o<sup>r</sup> shipps.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Some of the ffactors appointed to weighe what is bought. <strong>35.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that there be the sufficientest and best aduiced of o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> appointed to weighe what is bought by weight on the land &amp; to keepe a pfect Booke thereof, that the same may be nightlie conferred w<sup>th</sup> the Pursars Booke, whereby error<sup>s</sup> may be the better found out.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To bringe some of the Dollors the Hollenders carrie thither. <strong>36.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> bringe or send by the first shipps 8 or 10 <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-351" data-page="351" data-folio="259" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n350_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 259">[p. 259]</button> of the Dollers or Royalls w<sup>ch</sup> the Hollenders carrie thither.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Against excessiue drinkinge and banquettinge. <strong>37.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item we vtterlie forbidd any excessiue drinkeing feastinge or banquetinge abourd the shipps at Sea, or on land it being a greate wast &amp; Consumpc̃on of victualls and wyne, the cause of many diseases &amp; disorders in the Companie, &amp; therefore the same to be spent w<sup>th</sup> more discrec̃on and sparinge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To matt the shipps <strong>38.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item remember to matt the inside of the shipps before you put any spice vnto<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-040" href="#sec-07-fn-040" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-040" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> them especiallie where you lade Cloues Pepp Nutmegge and Mace, &amp; be carefull to make choise of the Nutmegge that they be lardg and sound, &amp; at the lading thereof doe not lyme them too much for that doth burne them,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-041" href="#sec-07-fn-041" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-041" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> &amp; beware yo<sup>u</sup> lay the Mace in Canisters in some fitt place by it selfe, that it be not spoyled by the heate of the other spices, takeing good heede to buie that w<sup>ch</sup> is bright &amp; not withered nor red nor darke browne Colored, &amp; such Cloues as shall be free of dust, the w<sup>ch</sup> those people vse to putt amongest them for their advantage in the weight.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To sprinkle the Cloues wth water if need be <strong>39.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item yf necessitie drawe you at the ladinge of the Cloues for better setlinge of them to cast water vpon them, be suer that it be but sprinklinge w<sup>th</sup> some thinge for the purpose, like a wateringe pott for that too much wett in o<sup>r</sup> Cloues the last voyadge brought vs greate damadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To settle afactorie &amp; where if need be &amp; where Comodities are sould better cheepe for gould then for money &amp;c: <strong>40.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that if it be thought meete that a ffactorie be setled at Banda, Tornato or Tedore the better to advise the Companie for trade as was form<sup>r</sup>lie thought fitt in the Comission geven to m<sup>r</sup> Kealinge &amp; m<sup>r</sup> Hawkins in the last Voyadge<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-042" href="#sec-07-fn-042" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-042" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> you may leaue some to that purpose w<sup>th</sup> some small stocke if vpon due considerac̃on thereof at yo<sup>r</sup> being there, it shall be soe thought fitt. And seeing that by former Accompte it appeth that those of Luntor and Nere did sell their Cloues &amp; Mace much better cheepe, to receiue their paym<sup>t</sup> in gould, then in Royalls namelie the Bahare of Nutmegge for 9 Royalls &amp; the Bahare of Mace for <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-352" data-page="352" data-folio="260" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n351_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 260">[p. 260]</button> 90 Royalls in sylver: But payinge w<sup>th</sup> gould the Price of Nutmegge but 7 Royalls &amp; of Mace but 70. We therefore thinke it needfull that all meanes be vsed to sell o<sup>r</sup> Comodities for Barter or gould &amp; to finde out such place where gould may be had as especiallie as we are informed at the Iland of ☞ Nouaginnye<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-043" href="#sec-07-fn-043" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-043" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> or ells where.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To giue the ffactors chardge often of what they are to obserue by this Comission. <strong>41.</strong></aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">what o<sup>r</sup> ffactors are to procure of the Cheneses &amp;c: <strong>42.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that the Generall &amp; Captaines geue often notice to the ffactors &amp; others what they are to obserue by this o<sup>r</sup> Comission w<sup>th</sup> espetiall chardge to the ffactors to make diligent inquirie of what Comodities are to be bought or bartered for in each Iland, &amp; what English m<sup>r</sup>chandize may be there vented, &amp; of what kinde and Colors most vendable Item it is to be remembred that o<sup>r</sup> ffactors at Bantam doe their best endevors to pcure the Cheneses to bring from Cheney sleaue and sowing silke of the Color and Syces<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-044" href="#sec-07-fn-044" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-044" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> we haue now deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to yo<sup>u</sup> the Generall as alsoe some rawe silke that we may fall into some trade w<sup>th</sup> them &amp; see yf they can sell any of o<sup>r</sup> English Cloth to them, that they may be brought to thuse thereof.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">An Invoice. <strong>43</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item for the quantities of money &amp; m<sup>r</sup>chandize put into each shipp we referr you to o<sup>r</sup> Invoice deliu<sup>r</sup>ed you herew<sup>th</sup>, w<sup>th</sup> the pticuler sorte Coullors &amp; prices w<sup>th</sup> paterns of all sorte whereby to make sale as is form<sup>r</sup>lie made menc̃on of in the 17<sup>th</sup> Article.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">noe voluntarie men or other to goe but such as shall be allowed by the Companie. <strong>44.</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item whereas heretofore the Companie haue receiued greate wronge by the Gen<sup>r</sup>all &amp; Captaines, m<sup>rs</sup> &amp; Mariners admittance of voluntarie men, to goe in o<sup>r</sup> shipps, we hereby giue an espetiall chardge vnto you o<sup>r</sup> Generall Captaines &amp; m<sup>rs</sup>: that you suffer none such to goe alonge w<sup>th</sup> you w<sup>ch</sup> put the Companie to chardge beside many other Inconvenience, nor any but such as shall be allowed of by o<sup>r</sup> direction &amp; whose names are entred into o<sup>r</sup> Booke.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The placeing or</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">leaueinge of the ffactors at the discrecon of the Genrall &amp; what the are to doe being left. 45.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">And yf you shall see cause to leaue any of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors there we doe hereby order that the appointm<sup>t</sup> of them to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-353" data-page="353" data-folio="261" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n352_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 261">[p. 261]</button> recide in those places shall be at the direcc̃on and discrec̃on of you the Generall wherein we hope You will haue that Considerac̃on as to make choise of the fittest w<sup>ch</sup> shall be found able and meete to deserue that trust of ymployment neither lett o<sup>r</sup> placeing them in the seu<sup>r</sup>all shipps as they are placed be any rule to you or any ground to them, to inioye those places of ymploym<sup>t</sup>, otherwise then they shall be found fitt and deserue, and o<sup>r</sup> further order is that you giue espetiall chardge to such as shall be by you left there to carrie themselues verie kindlie w<sup>th</sup> those People, for that the Contrarie as we are informed hath beene hurtfull vnto vs, &amp; that some of o<sup>r</sup> mens vnktnde &amp; churlish dealinge towarde them did form<sup>r</sup>lie make many of those People refraine to deale w<sup>th</sup> vs, &amp; goe to the Hollenders. And o<sup>r</sup> desier is that such as shall be left there doe there best endevo<sup>rs</sup> to finde out trade at Mesopotania<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-045" href="#sec-07-fn-045" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-045" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> in the pte of Bengalla Cheremendall S<sup>t</sup> Thome or any other places fitt for trade. And to take good obseruac̃on what English Comodities as Iron, Leade, Tynn, steele, Clothes, kersies, and saies w<sup>th</sup> their seu<sup>r</sup>all kinde and Colo<sup>rs</sup> are there best in request &amp; their values: As alsoe what Comodities are there to be bought as namelie Pintadoes &amp; such like for the Molloccos &amp; likewise Booke Calicutte and Lawnes called Casa and such other thinge as may serue for England, where the first sorte will serue best w<sup>th</sup> Pearles, stoanes Muske Bezar, and such like w<sup>th</sup> their seu<sup>r</sup>all prices, wherein we wish o<sup>r</sup> men to be ymployed, each man accordinge to his skill, the omission for seekeing out whereof all this while hath retourned to o<sup>r</sup> greate losse, and to the benefitt of the Hollenders whoe haue beene laborious and industrious therein, &amp; this inquirie to be made yf it be possible before yo<sup>r</sup> retourne to thend that at yo<sup>r</sup> retourne to England we may be effectuallie adu<sup>r</sup>ticed of all theis pticulars soe far as you may, &amp; of all other thinge for o<sup>r</sup> better direcc̃ons in the next voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Not to ymparte the trade to any but the Company 46.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item for that it is vnfitt &amp; inconvenient that o<sup>r</sup> trade be ympted vnto any but o<sup>r</sup> gen<sup>r</sup>all Companie it is expreslie ordered, &amp; soe the ffactors are to haue chardge not to write or aduice any their pticuler <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-354" data-page="354" data-folio="262" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n353_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 262">[p. 262]</button> freinde or others of the prices of any Comodities, either bought or sould But onelie in their gen<sup>r</sup>all łres to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To bring an Inventory of goodes debtes and moneys you shall leaue in those partes 47</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item that you o<sup>r</sup> Generall at yo<sup>r</sup> retourne from those pte for England doe bringe w<sup>th</sup> you vnto vs from thence an Inventorie of all o<sup>r</sup> goode debte &amp; moneys w<sup>ch</sup> you then shall happen to leaue remaineing in any those pte puttinge to this o<sup>r</sup> gen<sup>r</sup>all Accompte all guifte presente and gratifica~õns w<sup>ch</sup> shall be geven by any forreine Prince or Potentate vnto whome we haue now sent Presente or others.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To giue to the Admirall at Bantam a Present (if need be) 48</aside></div><p class="prose-p">And further o<sup>r</sup> direc~õn is that yo<sup>u</sup> o<sup>r</sup> Generall att yo<sup>r</sup> comeing to Bantam doe beside the łre and present to that kinge giue vnto the Admirall &amp; such other greate psonadge theare as o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> shall aduise you to haue beene freindlie to them, such present or gratifica~õn as shall be thought fitt, and by their further kinde dealeinge w<sup>th</sup> you they shall deserue.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A ffactorie at Priamon (if it may be). 49</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To bring some Tynn from Bantam &amp;c: 50</aside></div><p class="prose-p">And alsoe yf Convenientlie you may we wish that a ffactorie or at least some of o<sup>r</sup> people be appointed to recide at Priamon both for their better health and saffetie, &amp; for pcuring for vs pepp &amp; other Comodities: yf there shall be men and stocke there to ymploie &amp; you shall see iuste cause to doe the same Item o<sup>r</sup> direc~õn is that at yo<sup>r</sup> retourne from Bantam you doe bringe thence for the Companie some small quantitie of the East India Tynn for triall &amp; proofe thereof.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">What is to be done aboute the ffact of S<sup>r</sup> Edward Michelborne (if need be) 51.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item Touching the ffacte Comitted by S<sup>r</sup> Edward Michelborne<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-046" href="#sec-07-fn-046" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-046" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> though we and the rest of o<sup>r</sup> Companie knowe o<sup>r</sup> selues to be meerelie innocent therein, Yet if by any meanes we or o<sup>r</sup> goode should be dampnified thereby we wish you soe farr as saffelie you may to seeke to that Kinge and Magistrate for redresse and recompence thereof. The w<sup>ch</sup> yf you cannot obtaine then o<sup>r</sup> will is that you vse the benefitt geuen you in that behalfe, soe farr forth as you shall see cause &amp; be free of further danger.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-355"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="355" data-folio="263" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n354_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 263">[p. 263]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To buie of the lardgest pepper. 52</aside></div><p class="prose-p">And for that we finde best proffitt by the lardge sorte of Pepp both for the price that you pay aswell for thone as for theother, we pray you at any hand to buie soe much of the lardgest sorte as by any meanes you can gett, and let the same be put by it selfe into the shipp that it be not mingled w<sup>th</sup> any other.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">53.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item after the depture of the said Alexander Sharpie and Richard Rowles to diu<sup>r</sup>s Coaste as thone for England &amp; thother as we haue by this Comission ordered yf the said Richard Rowles shall happen to die (w<sup>ch</sup> god forbidd) then we doe appointe in his place for ordering and effectinge the busines by vs hereby Comitted vnto the said Richard Rowles his chardge vnto Richard Reaue ffacto<sup>r</sup>, But neu<sup>r</sup>thelesse if the said Richard Rowles shall happen to die before he shall pte Companie w<sup>th</sup> the said Alexander Sharpie for diu<sup>r</sup>s place as aforesaid, Then o<sup>r</sup> expresse will is that Willm Revett<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-047" href="#sec-07-fn-047" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-047" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> facto<sup>r</sup> be appointed in the place of the said Richard Rowles of Leiuetenant Generall in this present voyadge before any other. As alsoe by the death of the said Alexander Sharpie now o<sup>r</sup> Generall to succeede his place and chardge in the absence of the said Richard Rowles by death or otherwise vntill their meeteinge together againe in this voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">54.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Item now lastlie &amp; w<sup>the</sup> the first to be remembred we Comend vnto you all an espetiall care to haue yo<sup>r</sup> Eyes open to all deuices &amp; practises w<sup>ch</sup> may be wrought against you o<sup>r</sup> shipps and goode in eu<sup>r</sup>y place where you come keepinge a continuall watch, whereby you may be readie to prevent any mischeefe intended against you suspect all howe freindlie soeu<sup>r</sup> they seeme to be be redie still and stand vpon yo<sup>r</sup> guarde truste not yo<sup>r</sup> saffetie to the carelesse attendance of the Comon Mariners whoe will not loose their present pleasures or ease though the losse of yo<sup>r</sup> liues o<sup>r</sup> shipps and goode depend vpon it. And albeit we haue sett downe eu<sup>r</sup>y thinge what we remember &amp; desier to be done in pticular, Yet yf vpon due Consideracõn you finde cause to aulter any thinge, w<sup>ch</sup> we <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-356" data-page="356" data-folio="264" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n355_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 264">[p. 264]</button> heare haue sett downe, haueing w<sup>th</sup> good aduice resolued therevpon we referr the same to yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̃ons, &amp; for yo<sup>r</sup> better assistance &amp; warie proceedinges therein as alsoe in all other o<sup>r</sup> affaires we appointe Will̃m Revett Richard Reaue and Iohn Iourdan, if you see noe cause to the Contrarie &amp; such others as you the principall Comanders shall thinke fitt to Consider and Consult w<sup>th</sup> you the Generall of all thinge needfull, whome we wish you to imbrace &amp; vse w<sup>th</sup> a loueinge respect &amp; freindlie &amp; diligentlie to hearken to their Councells puttinge in execuc̃on what shall w<sup>th</sup> good deliberac̃on be soe agreed vpon: And soe we Comend you and yo<sup>r</sup> endevo<sup>rs</sup> to Gode prouidence whoe guide you w<sup>th</sup> his feare &amp; defend you from all dangers. Amen.</p></div><h2 class="sec-h1">Thinge deliu<sup>r</sup>ed by the Companie to m<sup>r</sup> Alexander Sharpie Generall in March 1607. Viz.</h2><div class="prose-unit"><ul class="editorial-list"><li>1. <span class="sc">Nprimis</span> This Comission of the Companie vnder their seale signed by the Secretarie.</li><li>2. Item 12 l̃res from his ma<sup>tie</sup>: &amp; the English of them to Prince in the Indies.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-048" href="#sec-07-fn-048" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-048" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></li><li>3. Item a saffe Conduct from his ma<sup>tie</sup>: vnder the priuie seale for passadge of o<sup>r</sup> men from the Indies ou<sup>r</sup>land w<sup>th</sup> a Coppie thereof in English.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-049" href="#sec-07-fn-049" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-049" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></li><li>4. Item a Comission to m<sup>r</sup> Sharpie Gen<sup>r</sup>all &amp; a duplicate to m<sup>r</sup> Richard Rowles Leiuetenant Gen<sup>r</sup>all vnder the greate seale of England, the w<sup>ch</sup> duplicatt is to remaine in his hande vntill they twoe depte Companie.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-050" href="#sec-07-fn-050" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-050" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup></li><li>5. Item a Coppie of the Priviledge obtayned by S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster at Dachem &amp; Sumatra to the Companie<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-051" href="#sec-07-fn-051" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-051" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> his obseruac̃on of tymes meete to be obserued in the East Indies<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-052" href="#sec-07-fn-052" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-052" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> And the Discripc̃on of the Ilande of Banda Amboyna Selebes o<sup>r</sup> Makasser Botton Seran w<sup>th</sup> the wares therein spent and bought &amp; their weighte <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-357" data-page="357" data-folio="265" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n356_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 265">[p. 265]</button> together w<sup>th</sup> c<sup>r</sup>teine direcc̃ons for saileing vpon the Coaste in the Indies.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-053" href="#sec-07-fn-053" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-053" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></li><li>6. A lre howe to deale w<sup>th</sup> Salbancke according to order of Courte the 5th of March 1607.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-054" href="#sec-07-fn-054" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-054" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></li><li>7. An order of Courte held the 11th of the said monneth of March, not to deale in any other mann<sup>r</sup> but by way of honest m<sup>r</sup>chandize<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-055" href="#sec-07-fn-055" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-055" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup></li><li>8. A lre to the ffactor<sup>s</sup> of the 3 voyadge<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-056" href="#sec-07-fn-056" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-056" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> w<sup>th</sup> a Coppie thereof &amp; of theirs form<sup>r</sup>lie sent the last yeare for better vnderstandinge what is to be pformed on their pte of the 4<sup>th</sup> voyadg w<sup>th</sup> a note of the manner of his priuate trade &amp; howe the same is to be registred &amp;c:</li><li>9. An Invoice of all moneys &amp; other thinge now sent: Carracters<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-057" href="#sec-07-fn-057" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-057" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> to write Overland (if need be) &amp; Beames weighte &amp; Scales &amp;c.</li></ul></div><h3 class="sec-h2">Thinge then deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to m<sup>r</sup> Richard Rowles Leiuetenante gen<sup>r</sup>all.</h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">I. Inprimis</span> the foresaid Comission of the Companie vnder their seale signed by the Secretory</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><ul class="editorial-list"><li>2 A Coppie of the foresaid Priuiledge obtayned by S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster at Dachem w<sup>th</sup> the obseruac̃on of tymes in the Indies &amp; the discripc̃on of Ilande wares weighte &amp; direcc̃on for saileinge vpon the Coaste of the Indies &amp;c: before mencioned.</li><li>3 An other lre to the ffactor<sup>s</sup> of the 3 voyadge for the more saffetie in deliu<sup>r</sup>y thereof accordinge to the supscripc̃on w<sup>th</sup> a note of the mann<sup>r</sup> of his priuate trade &amp; how the same is to be registred &amp;c: The Copies of all thinge before mencioned are form<sup>r</sup>lie recorded in this Register.</li></ul></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-358"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="358" data-folio="266" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n357_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 266">[p. 266]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">THE some of a Passe<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-058" href="#sec-07-fn-058" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-058" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to be procured for the East India Companie from the Grandsignio<sup>r</sup> to <strong>A B</strong> and <strong>C D</strong> Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> of his Citties of Aden and Mocca at the mouth of the red sea &amp; to all &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y other his Officers Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> and superio<sup>rs</sup> of any his Provinces Citties Townes Seas Rivers &amp; Bridge &amp;c: thereaboute, or ells where in any his Dominions.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The some of a saffe Conducte from the Grand signior.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">That whereas he and his Predicesso<sup>rs</sup> are and haue beene for the space of many yeares in league and amitie w<sup>th</sup> the mightie kinge of England &amp; the subiecte of that kingdome haue longe had and now haue free traffique and trade in Marchandizeing in his dominions and Countries through the Mediterranean Seas: And whereas some of the Subiecte of that kingdome (as he is informed[]) haueing w<sup>th</sup> their greate chardge and labo<sup>r</sup> found out atrade in Theast Indies, &amp; heareing of the fame &amp; wealth of some pte of his Countries in their passadge to the said Indies, are desirous to vizitt those place for thenlardginge of their said trade. And Considering how beneficiall the same may be to him &amp; his kingdomes, &amp; that such men in soe good an enterprize proffitable to humane Societie deserue not onelie praise and favo<sup>r</sup> but alsoe aide and helpe soe far as may be lawfullie &amp; convenientlie yealded. He hath beene pleased of his Princelie favo<sup>r</sup> to vouchsaffe them theis his łres of saffe Conducte and Comendačon Comanding the said <strong>A B.</strong> and <strong>C D.</strong> and all other his said Officers &amp;c: not onelie kindlie and loueinglie to enterteine and receiue the said Marchaunte subiecte of England, comeing or passinge throughe, or by any of his dominions assistinge and releiueinge them w<sup>th</sup> all thinge needfull for them selues, their men and shipps: But alsoe freelie to pmitt them by land or by Sea to goe or saile to goe forward, or retourne and as their occasion shall requier to remaine in any his Countries Citties or dominions, grantinge them such libtie of traffique and priuiledge as shall be reasonable, w<sup>th</sup>out giueing or sufferinge any lett hindrance Iniurie or molestačon to be offered or done vnto them, &amp; yealdinge vnto them such offices of benevolence &amp; humanitie as shall be convenient to be yealded honest men and strangers vndertakeinge soe longe and laborious a iourney handlinge their owne affaires &amp; behaueing them selues honestlie and peaceablie.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-359"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="359" data-folio="267" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n358_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 267">[p. 267]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To o<sup>r</sup> verie loueing freind m<sup>r</sup> Hum: Smyth m<sup>r</sup>chant at Hull.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-059" href="#sec-07-fn-059" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-059" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> o<sup>r</sup> hartie Comendačóns &amp;c: fforasmuch as we haue an intent to buie the shipp at Hull called the m<sup>r</sup>chaunt Royall soe as shee vpon veiwe may be found fitting for vs in all respectę aswell in her price as goodnes: &amp; vnderstanding that by some yo<sup>r</sup> affaires, you now haue occasion to be at Hull or thereaboutę: we haue thought yt good and haue soe concluded to send the bearer hereof m<sup>r</sup> Robert Earle vnto you a man skilfull in shippinge to thend that by o<sup>r</sup> instrucčóns giuen him (a coppie whereof we send you here inclosed) &amp; yo<sup>r</sup> good direcčóns, a diligent veiwe and inquirie may be taken of her, both for her price, goodnes, strength, breadth and fitnes for o<sup>r</sup> seruice, as for o<sup>r</sup> saffe inioyinge of her &amp;c: Wherefore we pray you to make ymployment of the said m<sup>r</sup> Earle herein, &amp; to giue him yo<sup>r</sup> helping hand for the pformance thereof in the best manner you may for the good of the Company. And further we doe requier you (yf you w<sup>th</sup> the advice of the said m<sup>r</sup> Earle) shall soe finde the said shipp in all respectę as aforesaid fitt for o<sup>r</sup> service to vse the best meanes you cann to buie her for thuse of this Companie soe as you may haue her deliu<sup>r</sup>ed here at London w<sup>th</sup> all thingę now belonging vnto her vnder or for the some of seaven hundred poundę at the most for the cheeffest man of abilitie and otherwise w<sup>th</sup> whome you are to deale for sale and securitie of her we conceiue to be m<sup>r</sup> Ioseph ffeild a man not vnknowne vnto you as we vnderstand But yf you cann not bargaine for her vnder or at that price at the most then we pray you to c<sup>r</sup>tifie vs of the pticulars thereof by the said m<sup>r</sup> Earle or by some more speedie <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-360" data-page="360" data-folio="268" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n359_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 268">[p. 268]</button> Messenger that vpon receipt of yo<sup>r</sup> łres we may resolue what to doe in the same. And thus not doubting of yo<sup>r</sup> care and kindnes we comitt you to the tui<span class="sc">c</span>õn of Thalmightie London the 20<sup>th</sup> of Aprill 1608.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> loueing freinde the Adventurers of theast India Company in the 4<sup>th</sup> voyadge.</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2">The Twenteth of Aprill 1608.</h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">A remembraunce for m<sup>r</sup> Robert Earle to be sent to Hull about veiwing of the shipp there to be sould called the m<sup>r</sup>chant Royall.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-060" href="#sec-07-fn-060" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-060" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">INPRIMIS you are w<sup>th</sup>all convenient expedi<span class="sc">c</span>õn to goe for Kingston vpon Hull or to Beverley or theareaboute to m<sup>r</sup> Humfrey Smyth of London m<sup>r</sup>chaunt at what place you may there first finde him &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ing him theis o<sup>r</sup> łres you are to take and ffollowe what direc<span class="sc">c</span>õns he shall giue you in this busines.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item yo<sup>u</sup> comeinge thither and findinge (w<sup>th</sup> the advice of the said m<sup>r</sup> Smyth) that the said shipp may be for o<sup>r</sup> seruice, you are to make diligent search and veiwe of her, of what strength she is of, what crosse beames, futtick Riders,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-061" href="#sec-07-fn-061" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-061" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> bottume riders &amp; needfull stannders<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-062" href="#sec-07-fn-062" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-062" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> &amp;c: she hath in her: whereby yt may be the better conceiued what the cost will be to make her fitt for o<sup>r</sup> voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item you are c<sup>r</sup>teinelie to learne her burthen, her breadth her length: how much water she draweth in her ballast, &amp; howe much full laden, yf she be any way weakened by the longe lyinge not ymployed: her adge, &amp; yf all her tymbers be sounde.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item you are to vnderstand &amp; see howe she is fitted w<sup>th</sup> good maste, sailes, yarde, Cables &amp; other furniture.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-361"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="361" data-folio="269" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n360_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 269">[p. 269]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item you are to make inquirie howe she is Condicioned in her saileing &amp; steridge as otherwise: &amp; alsoe what ordinance she hath, of weight and length &amp;c: &amp; of all other thinge needfull.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item to learne her lowest price, &amp; in that price what ordinance maste sailes yarde Cables &amp; other thinge are to be sould w<sup>th</sup> her and being bought howe she may be brought hither &amp; at whose chardge &amp; what good securitie may be had for enioyinge of her, &amp; by whome yt will be geven.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Lastlie you are to make an Inventorie of all the premisses in writeinge the better to informe vs at yo<sup>r</sup> retourne, in what state she is, what she hath &amp; what she wanteth &amp; the same being pformed you are to retourne by the first meanes you may: But yf before yo<sup>r</sup> comeing thither yt shall fall out that m<sup>r</sup> Humfrey Smyth shall be retourned thence for London then you are to followe theis yo<sup>r</sup> direccons w<sup>th</sup>out him wherein you are to vse all care &amp; expedicon &amp; Retourne.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Bill of Adventure to mr. Groues</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">this is corrected &amp; <sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-064" href="#sec-07-fn-064" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-064" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> made as it was deliured to his wieff</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">HEAREAS</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-063" href="#sec-07-fn-063" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-063" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> we the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Company of Marchaunte of London trading into the East Indies Haue interteyned and hired Phillipp de Graeffe of the precinct of S<sup>t</sup> Katherines neare to Tower of London Mariner to goe as principall m<sup>r</sup> in one of the twoe shipps now latelie by vs prepared &amp; sent in o<sup>r</sup> fourth voyadge for those pte. And haue allowed and paied him for his ymployment in the said voyadge before his depture hence theis seu<sup>r</sup>all somes following Viz. The some of One hundred Twentie and ffyve pounde for his prepacon to sea: The some of Twentie pounde for fower monnethes Harborrow wage after the rate of ffyve <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-362" data-page="362" data-folio="270" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n361_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 270">[p. 270]</button> pounde p̃ monneth: The some of ffortie shillinge for Boate hier: And further vpon his intreatie haue lent and ymprested vnto him the some of Eeighteene pounde ouʳ &amp; aboue the somes formerlie giuen him, the wᶜʰ some of Eighteene pounde is onelie to be defalked out of such proffitt of Adventure as hereafter is granted him. Now knowe yee that we the said Gouʳnoʳ &amp; Company, for further allowance &amp; recompence of the good service travell and paines wᶜʰ he the said Phillipp de Graeffe faithfullie p̃myseth to to his best endeuoʳˢ to p̃forme in the said voyadge both outwarde &amp; homewarde for the good of the said Company Doe by theis presente for vs &amp; oʳ Successoʳˢ promyse and agree to giue and allowe vnto him the said Phillipp de Graeffe his Executoʳˢ Administratoʳˢ or Assignes, vpon the gaine &amp; proffitt wᶜʰ god shall send by the retourne of the same voyadge (the principall Adventure thereof, &amp; all chardge of the voyadge goode &amp; mʳchandizes of what kinde soeuʳ both by land or water first borne &amp; deducted[])] Soe much gaine as ffyve hundred and fyftie pounde will produce that is to saie such rate &amp; pffitt as yf he had beene an Adventurer of the some of ffyve hundred and ffyftie pounde, &amp; as yf he had put in soe much readie money in stocke in the said voyadge. The wᶜʰ rate and proporc̃on of proffitt is to be paid vnto him the said Phillip de Graeff his Executoʳˢ Adˢ or Assignes in such manner and forme · as the gaine and proffitt of the said voyadge shall be paid to all other the Adventurers genʳallie, vpon the some of ffyue hundred and ffyftie pounde by them adventured.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">But if he happen to die before the retourne of the said shipps from the said East Indies, or those other place whither they they are directed, hauinge liued &amp; Contynued in the said voiadge vntill the shipps, or shipp wherein he shall serue shall arriue &amp; come to such porte in those parte, at wᶜʰ it shalbe founde fitt to lade the same: Then the said gaine p̃porcioned of the said rate of 550<sup>li</sup> aduenture to be paid to his Executoʳˢ Adˢ or Assignes, as it should haue been paid vnto him, if he had retourned wᵗʰ the said shipps. Prouided alwaies that if [he] decease before the shipps shall arriue to such porte in those pte at wᶜʰ they are to take in their ladinge. That then the moneys imprested &amp; deliuʳed him before his goinge to Sea shalbe vnto him, in full recompence of his <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-363" data-page="363" data-folio="271" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n362_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 271">[p. 271]</button> entertainem<sup>t</sup> formerlie agreed vppon for the voiadge. And vppon theis Condicõns &amp; pviso before menc̃oed we the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Companie vnder o<sup>r</sup> Comon Seale, haue giuen vnto the said Phillip de Graeff this p̄nte Bill of Contract &amp; aduenture dated the 20<sup>th</sup> day of Iune 1608 And in the yeare of the reigne of o<sup>r</sup> sou<sup>r</sup>eigne lord Iames Kinge of England ffrance &amp; Ireland the Sixt &amp; of Scotland the one &amp; fforteth.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Bill of an Aduenturer of 550<sup>li</sup>, in the 4<sup>th</sup> voiadge, to an Adventurer vnder him</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Wheareas</span> Wiłłm Iennyngꬵ Citizen and             of London one of the Adventurers and one of the Brethren of the Company of Marchauntꬵ of London trading into the East Indies Hath not onelie Adventured the some of 100<sup>li</sup> vnder me Wiłłm Bonham Citizen and Vintener of London &amp; alsoe one of the Adventurers &amp; one of the said Company of m<sup>r</sup>chantꬵ tradinge into the East Indies in their fourth voyadge for those p̄tꬵ: But hath likewyse trulie paid the said some of 100<sup>li</sup> to me the said William Bonham as appeth by the register of the said Company the 4th of March 1607.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-065" href="#sec-07-fn-065" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-065" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Now knowe yee that I the said Wiłłm Bonham doe by theis p̄ntꬵ p̄myse and agree to &amp; w<sup>th</sup> the said Wiłłm Iennyngꬵ That I the said Wiłłm Bonham my Executor<sup>rs</sup> &amp; Administrator<sup>rs</sup> shall &amp; will vpon the retourne of the shipps or either of them sett out by the said Company in their said 4th voyadge or w<sup>th</sup>in Convenient tyme after their said retourne in the same deliu<sup>r</sup> vnto the said Wiłłm Iennyngꬵ his Executor<sup>rs</sup> Administrator<sup>rs</sup> or Assignes a true and iust accompt and paym<sup>t</sup> of the said some of 100<sup>li</sup> soe by him adventured vnder me &amp; of the proffitt &amp; benefitt w<sup>ch</sup> god shall send thereof ratablelie and accordinge to the gen<sup>r</sup>all distribucõn proporcõn and allotm<sup>t</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall be allotted disposed and geven to me the said Wiłłm Bonham &amp; to all other the seu<sup>r</sup>all Adventurers of 550<sup>li</sup> in the said fourth voyadge proporcionallie to each mans seu<sup>r</sup>all Adventure. In witnes whereof I haue herevnto sett my hand and seale the 4th day of Iulie 1608. And in the Yeare of the Reign of &amp;c:</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-364"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="364" data-folio="272" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n363_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 272">[p. 272]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Bill of Adventure of 550<sup>li</sup> in the fourth voyadge to an Adventurer vnder him the Bill on totherside of this leafe<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-066" href="#sec-07-fn-066" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-066" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> not being allowed.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">This</span> Bill or writeinge witnesseth That whereas I Henrie Bridgman Citizen and Leatherseller of London, Haue vndertaken to adventure as one of the Brethren &amp; Adventurers of the Company of Marchaunte tradinge to the East Indies the some of 600<sup>li</sup> of lawfull money of England in this their p̄nte intended expedĩcõn or fourth voyadge to the East Indies in the good shipps called the Asseñcõn &amp; the Vnion. And whereas Nicholas Skynner Citizen and Cutlar of London, &amp; alsoe one of the Brethren of the said Company of Marchaunte trading to the said East Indies, hath alreadie trulie paid to me the said Henry Bridgman the some of 150<sup>li</sup> of like lawfull money of England being one 4<sup>th</sup> parte of the said some or stocke before mencioned, w<sup>ch</sup> said some he the said Nicholas Skinner is Contented to Adventure vnder me in the said fourth voyadge in such manner as all other Vnderadventurers haue done. Knowe ye therefore That I the said Henrie Bridgman for me my Executors and Administrators &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y of vs doe couenant promyse and graunte to and w<sup>th</sup> the said Nicholas Skinner his Execū &amp; Administr &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y of them by theis pnte That yt shall and may be lawfull to and for the said Nicholas Skynner his Execū Administr &amp; Ass<sup>s</sup> &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y of them vpon the retourne of the ships or either of them sett out in this said fourth Voyadge or w<sup>th</sup>in Convenient tyme after their said retourne in the same to haue recciue &amp; enioye to his and their propp vse &amp; behoofe out of my said stocke &amp; adventure of 600<sup>li</sup> the some of One hundred and ffyftie by him the said Nicholas Skynner adventured vnder me in the said fourth voyadge as aforesaid together w<sup>th</sup>all such benefitt proffitt encrease and gaine as god shall send thereof ratablie from tyme to tyme according to the gen<sup>r</sup>all distribũcõn propor̃cõn and allotm<sup>t</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall be allotted disposed and giuen to me the said Henry Bridgman &amp; all other the Aduenturers in the said voiadge, proportionallie to eache mans seu<sup>r</sup>all Aduenture. And furder that I the said Henrie Bridgman my execū &amp; Ađs &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y of vs shall and will at all tymes &amp; from tyme to tyme hereafter vpon reasonable request to vs or any of vs made by the said Nicholas <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-365" data-page="365" data-folio="273" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n364_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 273">[p. 273]</button> Skynner his Executor<sup>s</sup> Admī or Ass<sup>s</sup> make him the said Nicholas Skynner his Execū &amp; Admī acquainted, w<sup>th</sup> all &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y the proceedinge in the said voyadge, w<sup>ch</sup> shall Concerne the takeing out &amp; receiueing of the said some of 150<sup>li</sup> w<sup>th</sup> the proffitte thereof as amplie as shall any way come to the knowledge of me the said Henrie Bridgman my Executor<sup>s</sup> and Ađs w<sup>th</sup>out fraude or guile. In witnes whereof I haue &amp;c.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Bill of Adventure for the 4<sup>th</sup> voyadge</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Bill of Adventure to the third voyadge.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">WHEREAS<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-067" href="#sec-07-fn-067" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-067" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> <strong>A B</strong> Citizen and &amp;c . . . . . . one of the Adventurers and one of the Brethren of the Companie of Marchaunt<em>e</em> of London tradinge into the East Indies hath not onelie sett downe for his Adventure w<sup>th</sup> the said Company to the some of 550<sup>li</sup> in their 4<sup>th</sup> voyadge to the East Indies by the Cape <em>Bona spei:</em> in the good shipps called the Assenc̃on &amp; the Vnion But hath alsoe trulie paied the said some of 550<sup>li</sup> for his Adventure to Richard Atkinson Casheare to the said Adventurers in the 4<sup>th</sup> voyadge. We therefore the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Company doe by this o<sup>r</sup> present Bill of Adventure promyse and agree to and w<sup>th</sup> the said <strong>A B.</strong> That we the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Comp: and o<sup>r</sup> Successor<sup>s</sup> shall and will vpon the retourne of the ships called the Assenc̃on &amp; Vnion or either of them sett out by vs in this present fourth voyadge or w<sup>th</sup>in Convenient tyme after their said retourne in the same deliu<sup>r</sup> vnto the said <strong>A B</strong> ..... his Execū Administ: or Assignes atrue and iust Accompt and paym<sup>t</sup> of such stocke benefitt and proffitt of stocke as god shall send vpon the said voyadge according to the gen<sup>r</sup>all distribuc̃on pporc̃on and allotm<sup>t</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall be allotted dispôsed and given to all &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y the seu<sup>r</sup>all Adventurers except &amp; alwaies reserued out of the said some or stocke before mencioned for and to thuse of the right owners of the same all such somes w<sup>ch</sup> shall in due tyme iustlie appeare to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-366" data-page="366" data-folio="274" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n365_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 274">[p. 274]</button> the said Company to be adventured vnder the said 𝕬 𝕭...... by any vnderadventurer in the said fourth voyadge togither w<sup>th</sup>all the proffitte &amp; benefitte thereof w<sup>th</sup>out fraude or guile. In witnes whereof &amp;c: <span class="sc">wheareas</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-068" href="#sec-07-fn-068" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-068" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> 𝕴 𝕸.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-069" href="#sec-07-fn-069" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-069" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> citizen and grocer of London one of the Adventurers &amp; one of the Brethren of the Company of Marchaunte of London trading into the East Indies hath not onelie sett downe for his Adventure w<sup>th</sup> the said Company the some of 150 pounde in their third voyadge to the East Indies by the Cape <em>Bona spei</em>: But hath alsoe trulie paid the said some of 150<sup>li</sup> for his Adventure to Thomas ffarrington Threr of the said Company. We therefore the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Company doe by this o<sup>r</sup> p̄nte Bill of Adventure p̄myse and agree to and w<sup>th</sup> the said 𝕴 𝕸. That we the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Company &amp; o<sup>r</sup> Successo<sup>rs</sup> shall and will vpon the retourne of the shipps or any of them sett out by vs in this p̄nte third voyadge or w<sup>th</sup>in Convenient tyme after their said retourne in the same deliu<sup>r</sup> vnto the said 𝕴 𝕸. his Execū Adminī or Ass<sup>s</sup> a true and iust accompt and paym<sup>t</sup> of such stocke benefitt and <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-367" data-page="367" data-folio="275" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n366_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 275">[p. 275]</button> proffitt of stocke as god shall send vpon the said voyadge according to the gen<sup>r</sup>all distribũcõn propor̃cõn and allotm<sup>t</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall be allotted disposed and giuen to all &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y the seu<sup>r</sup>all Adventurers in the said voyadge proporcionally to their seu<sup>r</sup>all Adventures. In witnes whereof we haue herevnto annexed o<sup>r</sup> Comon seale the 26th day of Aprill 1608. And in the yeare of the reigne of o<sup>r</sup> sou<sup>r</sup>eigne Lord kinge Iames of England ffraunce and Ireland the Sixt &amp; of Scotland the One and fforteth.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Examined by me Thomas ffarrington</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Sealed w<sup>th</sup> the Comon seale of the Companie in the pn̄te of me W<sup>m</sup> Leighton<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-070" href="#sec-07-fn-070" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-070" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Secretarie Copia</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Bills</span> of Adventure as appeth on the other side of this leaffe geven by the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Company of East India Marchaunte to the seuerall Adventurers in their third voyadge for those pte w<sup>th</sup> their seu<sup>r</sup>all somes, &amp; the same vnder written by m<sup>r</sup> ffarrington as by him examined before thensealeinge thereof. The w<sup>ch</sup> are as ffolloweth viz. To</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To m<sup>r</sup> {</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To m<sup>r</sup></aside></div><div class="ledger-table-block" id="tbl-014"><div class="table-toolbar"><span class="table-id-badge">TBL-014 · FOLIO 367</span><span class="table-csv-links"><a href="/downloads/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/datasets/tables/tbl-014_p0367.csv" download>Diplomatic CSV</a> · <a href="/downloads/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/datasets/tidy/tbl-014_p0367.tidy.csv" download>Tidy CSV</a></span></div><div class="ledger-table-wrap" role="region" aria-label="Statistical table" tabindex="0"><table class="ledger-table"><tbody><tr class="row-data"><td>Morris</td><td>Abbott . for</td><td>. . .</td><td class="num">200<sup>li</sup></td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>William</td><td>Adderley</td><td>. . .</td><td class="num">125</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>William</td><td>Allen</td><td>. . .</td><td class="num">100</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>To m<sup>r</sup> }<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-071" href="#sec-07-fn-071" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-071" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Raphe</td><td>Allen</td><td>. .
