282]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The lo: Thrers warrant to y<sup>e</sup> ffarmors for transportacon of 6000li parte of 2000li<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-076" href="#sec-07-fn-076" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-076" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> form<sup>r</sup>lie granted the Company by Patent from his matie:</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER my harty Comendačons, Whereas the Marchantes tradinge to the East Indies, by his Ma<sup>te</sup> warraunt vnder the greate Seale of England beareinge date the Eight daye of february: In the v<sup>th</sup> yeare of his highnes raigne had licence to carry foorth: 20000<sup>li</sup>: sterlinge of forreine Coyne, and havinge as yett transported onely: 14000<sup>li</sup>: Theis are to will and require yo<sup>u</sup> to suffer them to transporte the said somme in a Shippe called the Expedičon, accordinge to his Ma<sup>te</sup> pleasure, signified in the said letters pattente: And for so doinge this by vertue thereof shalbe yo<sup>r</sup> warrant: from <em>Whitehall:</em> the xj<sup>th</sup> of Aprill: 1609:</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> Lovinge freind <em>R. SALISBURY</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Coppie of his maties Comission to Capt: David Middleton ye 11th of Aprill. 1609</aside></div><p class="prose-p">To my very lovinge freindes the officers of his Ma<sup>te</sup> Customes outwarde in the Port of London, and to eu<sup>r</sup>y of them to whome it may apperteine JAMES<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-077" href="#sec-07-fn-077" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-077" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> by the grace of God kinge of England Scotland ffraunce and Ireland defender of the faith &amp;c: To o<sup>r</sup> trustie and welbeloued David Middleton whereas diu<sup>r</sup>s of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge Subiecte at their owne Adventures, Coste and chardge, aswell for the honor of this o<sup>r</sup> Realme of England, as for the increase &amp; advancem<sup>t</sup> of trade and m<sup>r</sup>chandize, w<sup>th</sup>in the same, Haue form<sup>r</sup>lie sett forth fower voyadge to the East Indies, w<sup>th</sup> c<sup>r</sup>teine shipps and Pinnaces by way of m<sup>r</sup>chandizinge, by w<sup>ch</sup> shipps and Pinnasses they haueinge discou<sup>r</sup>ed and begunn to settle a trade in some parte of the said East Indies &amp; their shipps beinge retourned in three of their former voyadge from thence, laden w<sup>th</sup> sundrie m<sup>r</sup>chandize brought from <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-375" data-page="375" data-folio="283" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n374_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 283">[p. 283]</button> those ptę, They the said Marchauntę intendinge to feequent those Countries of the East Indies by contynueinge the trade alreadie discouʳed and begun. And indevoringe further discouʳy of trade of those partę for the more ample vent of the natiue Comodities of oʳ kingdomes And retourneing from the place of their discouʳy such necessarie comodities as shallbe of spetiall vse &amp; benefitt both to Vs and oʳ Subiectę &amp; haue now prepared a cʳteine shipp to the said East Indies: <em>A<span class="sc">nd wheareas</span></em> the said Marchauntę haue chosen yoᵘ the said David Middleton, to be principall Gouʳnoʳ or Generall of all the Marchauntę, Mariners and other oʳ Subiectę wᶜʰ are or shall be shipped in the said Shipp, we gratiouslie favoringe their intended voyadge and approueinge and shallowinge of their choise of you to the same gouʳnmᵗ, beinge desierous to furnish yoᵘ wᵗʰall fitt and Convenient power and authoritie to rule and gouʳne all &amp; euʳy our Subiectę imployed in this voyadge, by a due obedience to be by them yealded Vnto you, in the obserueinge and executeinge of all such good orders &amp; Constitucõns as yoᵘ shall thinke Convenient to ordeine and appointe for the furtherance of the said voyadge to the honor of Vs and oʳ kingdomes, and the advancemᵗ of the said trade, <em>D<span class="sc">oe</span></em> hereby straightlie chardge and Comand all &amp; euʳy pson and psonns imployed vsed or shipped in this Voyadge in the said Shipp, to giue all due obedience and respect Vnto yoᵘ during the said Voyadge &amp; to beare themselues one towardę another in all good order and quietnes for avoieding any occasion that might breede mutiny quarrells or dissençõn amongst them to the hindrance of the good successe wᶜʰ is to be hoped for by Godę providence of the said intended Voyadge <em>A<span class="sc">nd</span></em> in default of such dutie and obedience to be pformed towardę you for the correccõn and quenchinge of all such mutiny quarrells or dissençõn that shall or may be moved by the disorder evell disposicõn or pversnes of any the said psonns, <em>W<span class="sc">ee doe</span></em> hereby authorize you the said David Middleton duringe the said Voyadge or during soe longe tyme as yoᵘ shall lyue in the said Voyadge to chastice correct and ponish all offenders &amp; transgressors in that behalfe accordinge to the qualitie of their offenses wᵗʰ such punnishmᵗę as are comonlie vsed in all Armies at Sea, when they are not Capitall And for Capitall offences, as wilfull murther wᶜʰ is hatefull in the sight of God, or mutinie wᶜʰ is an offence that may tende to the ouʳthrowe of the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-376" data-page="376" data-folio="284" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n375_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 284">[p. 284]</button> said Voyadge, the same being trulie and iustlie proved against any of the pson or psons aforesaid, <em>WE DOE</em> hereby giue vnto yo<sup>u</sup> the said David Middleton duringe all the tyme of the said voyadge, or during soe longe tyme as yo<sup>u</sup> shall lyve in the said Voyadge full power &amp; authoritie to vse and putt in execucõn o<sup>r</sup> Lawe called Martiall Lawe in that behalfe: <em>AND THEIS</em> o<sup>r</sup> łres shalbe vnto you sufficient warrant and dischargdge for the doinge and executinge of all &amp; singuler the premisses: <em>A<span class="sc">ND</span></em> forasmuch as att this present tyme we are in amitie . w<sup>th</sup>all Christian Princes &amp; are vnwillinge that any our Subiecte should giue occasion of breach or hindrance of that league or amitie w<sup>ch</sup> we hould w<sup>th</sup> any o<sup>r</sup> Confederate freinde or Alleys. And because we are not ignorant of the emulacõn &amp; envy that doth accompany the discou<sup>r</sup>y of Countries and trade, &amp; of the quarrells and Contencõns w<sup>ch</sup> doe many tymes fall out betweene the subiecte of diu<sup>r</sup>s Princes, that meete the one w<sup>th</sup> theother in forraine &amp; farr remote Countries in the Course &amp; psecutinge of their discou<sup>r</sup>yes, &amp; being desierous that our subiecte should forbeare to moue or beginn any quarrell or Contencõn (Vppon the Subiecte of our Confederate freinde or Allies[]], either in their proceedinge or retourne vpon or from any of their Voyadge, <em>WE THERFORE</em> doe hereby straightlie chardge and Comand you the said David Middleton &amp; all other vnder yo<sup>r</sup> gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> that neither in yo<sup>r</sup> voyadge outwarde or homewarde or in any Country, Island, Porte, or place where yo<sup>u</sup> shall abide or come duringe the tyme of yo<sup>r</sup> beinge abroad out of o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes or dominions where you meete w<sup>th</sup> any the Subiecte of the kinge of Spaigne or of any other o<sup>r</sup> Confederate ffreinde or Allies, or of any other Nacõn or people their Shipps Vessells Goode or m<sup>r</sup>chandize yo<sup>u</sup> doe not attempt or goe aboute to sett vpon take or supprise their psonns Ships Vessells goode or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes or offer any iniury or discourtesie vnto them vnlesse yo<sup>u</sup> shall be by them first therevnto iustlie provoked, or driuen either in the iust defence of yo<sup>r</sup> owne psonns yo<sup>r</sup> Shipps Vessells Goode or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes either by any other their disturbance or hindrance whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> in yo<sup>r</sup> quiett course of trade either for recompence or recou<sup>r</sup>y of the psonns goode or m<sup>r</sup>chandize of any o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte that are alreadie in or neare the East Indies or otherwyse for any iust cause of yo<sup>r</sup> defence, as yo<sup>u</sup> will awnsweare the contrary at yo<sup>r</sup> vttermost prills.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-377"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="377" data-folio="285" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n376_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 285">[p. 285]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> wheareas we are informed that the said David Middleton hath sould in the East Indies, Three small peece of cast Iron Ordinance contrary to the Lawes of this o<sup>r</sup> Realme, for that the same was done vpon necessitie, &amp; for the good of the publique service, &amp; the releefe of such as were distressed in that voyadge. <em>W<span class="sc">e</span></em> are gratiouslie pleased to dispence w<sup>th</sup> him in that behalfe: And doe by theis presente pardon and remitt the said offence, and dischardge him the said David Middleton of and from all such penalties as by o<sup>r</sup> said Lawes may be inflicted vpon him for sellinge of the said Ordinance w<sup>th</sup>out o<sup>r</sup> licence first had and obteyned in that behalfe, Any Lawe statute or restrainte to the contrary in any wyse notw<sup>th</sup>standinge. In wittnes whereof we haue caused theis o<sup>r</sup> lres to be made Pattente: Witnes o<sup>r</sup>selfe at Westm̄ the 11<sup>th</sup> day of Aprill in the Seaventh yeare of o<sup>r</sup> Raigne of England ffraunce and Ireland, &amp; of Scotland the Twoe and fforteth.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">p̄ breve de privato sigillo:</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Coppin.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Coppie of the lres sent to the ffactors at Bantam in thexpedicon by Capt: David Middleton.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">After</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-078" href="#sec-07-fn-078" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-078" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> o<sup>r</sup> hartie Comendañons &amp;c Yo<sup>r</sup> letters of the xv<sup>th</sup> of Ianuary 1607<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-079" href="#sec-07-fn-079" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-079" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> by Cornelius Matalife together w<sup>th</sup> a briefe of the state of o<sup>r</sup> busines in yo<sup>r</sup> handlinge we haue receiued: As alsoe yo<sup>r</sup> last of the xv<sup>th</sup> of Iulie<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-080" href="#sec-07-fn-080" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-080" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> by Captaine David Middleton in the Consent: w<sup>ch</sup> shipp arriued here in Ianuary last (thanke be to god) in good saffetie, laden w<sup>th</sup> Cloues to o<sup>r</sup> very good contentm<sup>te</sup>, for w<sup>ch</sup> we haue had a good price &amp; present dispatch, Soe as we haue none remaineinge to sell: By him we alsoe receiued Henry Sydalls Iournall, wherein appeared what goode yo<sup>u</sup> sent in the said Shipp to the Molluccoes, what thereof was sould, what retorned to yo<sup>u</sup>, &amp; of Captaine Middletons whole proceedinge in the buyinge of the said Cloues: Alsoe one other Iournall of yo<sup>r</sup> accompte touchinge <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-378" data-page="378" data-folio="286" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n377_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 286">[p. 286]</button> o<sup>r</sup> busines in yo<sup>r</sup> handlinge since the same was deliu<sup>r</sup>ed over to yo<sup>u</sup> by Edmund Scott, vntill the first of Iulie last, and thereby rest satisfied w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> due pceedinge in accompte, albeit as yett we haue not examined the same: By yo<sup>r</sup> lres we alsoe pceiue that yo<sup>u</sup> borrowed 3000 Royalls of the Dutch Marchaunte to satisfie soe much taken vp of the Captaine and Company of the said shipp to supplie o<sup>r</sup> wante, for the ladinge of the said Cloues w<sup>ch</sup> we hope vpon the arriuall of the Dragon and Hector, or by some other meanes, yo<sup>u</sup> haue dulie repaid w<sup>th</sup>all thankfullnes, assureinge them further That we will hould their said kindnes in remembraunce, and be readie to requite the same whensoeu<sup>r</sup> occasion shalbe offred: We are very sorry that there was noe better dispatch found for o<sup>r</sup> Cloth at the Molluccoes, consideringe the same is much ympaired by the longe lieinge as yo<sup>u</sup> advertice vs, But we hope that the Dragon and the Hector arriued w<sup>th</sup> you shortlie after, And that by them the same will be vented, either at Banda or the Molluccos w<sup>ch</sup> God graunte we may heare: Moreou<sup>r</sup> we trust that yo<sup>u</sup> haue vsed diligence to putt of all other the remaine of o<sup>r</sup> goode at Bantam, recouered o<sup>r</sup> debte there, and ymployed the proceede thereof, accordinge to o<sup>r</sup> lres by the said twoe shipps<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-081" href="#sec-07-fn-081" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-081" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> If not we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to hasten the same.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ballance the Ac-comptes &amp;c:</aside></div><p class="prose-p">That thaccompt of that busines may be ballanced &amp; to ymploy what yo<sup>u</sup> haue remaineinge [(]not inuested into pepp) in Rawe silke of China called Lamkin, like vnto that sample w<sup>ch</sup> Iohn Saris sent vnto o<sup>r</sup> Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> S<sup>r</sup> Tho: Smyth: But take heed yo<sup>u</sup> buy not of the silke that is crosse reeled, &amp; called canton for that sorte is not here vendible.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-082" href="#sec-07-fn-082" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-082" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">silke called Lawkin</aside></div><p class="prose-p">The silke called Lawkin, is here well requested Therefore we pray yo<sup>u</sup> vse your best endevors to putt of o<sup>r</sup> english Cloth for that Comoditie, whereto as it seemeth by Robt Brownes lres the Chineses were willinge &amp; desierous, if yo<sup>u</sup> had beene furnished w<sup>th</sup> any w<sup>ch</sup> giueth vs good hope that those people, will fall to weare o<sup>r</sup> Cloth, Soe as we shall finde good vent for the same hereafter, &amp; haue better <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-379" data-page="379" data-folio="287" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n378_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 287">[p. 287]</button> meanes to maintaine an ampler trade there. ffor the better procureinge whereof, we haue now and will heerafter, send such Cloth as shall be true, both in substaunce and Couller, and soe yo<sup>u</sup> may assure them Moreou<sup>r</sup> we Vnderstand by m<sup>r</sup> Brownes letter that a trade may be had to <em>Siquedana</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-083" href="#sec-07-fn-083" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-083" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> for Diamonde whereof we would be glad to tast, and doe approue of his diligence in that and other adv<sup>r</sup>tisem<sup>te</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Diamondes Bezar stones &amp;ca:</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Indico</aside></div><p class="prose-p">We hope that the Dragon and Hector arriued w<sup>th</sup> you in Convenient tyme, and that yo<sup>u</sup> haue pceeded, to buy some Diamonde, Bezar stones, Muske &amp; other Comodities (whereof hitherto we haue had none) according to o<sup>r</sup> Comission, and lres sent by o<sup>r</sup> Generall m<sup>r</sup> W<sup>m</sup>: Keelinge, for w<sup>ch</sup> purpose we sent m<sup>r</sup> Marlowe a Ieweller to goe to such place for the buyinge thereof, as yo<sup>u</sup> should direct him: And yf neither m<sup>r</sup> Keelinge, nor m<sup>r</sup> Sharpie, haue entred into the trade of Cambaio, <em>Surat</em> or some other place of the maine, Then we would haue yo<sup>u</sup> to informe yo<sup>r</sup> selues howe strangers are intertayned at Suratt &amp; Cambaio: And beinge imbouldenned by their aduise we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to appointe twoe of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors such as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fitt to goe passengers in some Iuncke or other shippinge of that Country to Surat &amp; Cambaia w<sup>th</sup> such stocke as yo<sup>u</sup> thinke good, to beare chardge to and againe to buy such Comodities as they shall finde there as Indico, of Laher,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-084" href="#sec-07-fn-084" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-084" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> here worth viij<sup>s</sup> the pounde <em>Serchis</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-085" href="#sec-07-fn-085" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-085" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> and the best <em>Belondri</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-086" href="#sec-07-fn-086" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-086" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> worth v<sup>s</sup> the pounde the Courser sorte <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-380" data-page="380" data-folio="288" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n379_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 288">[p. 288]</button> will not requite the chardge and such other Comodities as they shall finde theare: Alsoe we would haue yo<sup>u</sup> procure the best favor yo<sup>u</sup> can w<sup>th</sup> the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and other principall men of each place, yo<sup>u</sup> come to, whereby yo<sup>u</sup> may be able to enforme vs what hope we may haue of trade heereafter to such place, and yo<sup>u</sup> learne what Comodities of o<sup>rs</sup>, kinde, sorte, &amp; Cullo<sup>rs</sup> will vent there, as namelie what Clothes, karsies ppetuanoes or other woollen Comodities &amp; what Cullo<sup>rs</sup> are in best and worse request leade, Tynn, Iron, steele, muskette, Calliuers,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-087" href="#sec-07-fn-087" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-087" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Dagge,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-088" href="#sec-07-fn-088" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-088" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> swordblade, headpeece, &amp;c: and alsoe what Commodities they finde there fitt to serue for the Molluccos or other easterne pte, or to bringe home for England w<sup>th</sup> such other informac̃ons as experience there shall enable yo<sup>u</sup> w<sup>th</sup>: Alsoe to send them to any other Citties or places of cheefest trade in those parte, &amp; there to learne the matters before mentioned, and not to lie still at Bantam as yf yo<sup>u</sup> were tyed and bound to that Towne onelie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><span class="sc">Thomas Emmesworth</span></aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">spices from Banda &amp;c</aside></div><p class="prose-p">we haue sent in this Ship a younge man one Thomas Emmesworth<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-089" href="#sec-07-fn-089" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-089" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> a man of good reputac̃on, and soe we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to accompt of him, whoe hath beene brought vp some tyme in Turkey and hath verie good skill in <em>Indico</em>, of all sorte of silke, &amp; other Comodities vsuallie bought in Turkey, whose advise therein we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to followe Alsoe we haue sent in this Shipp three other servaunte, whoe albeit they haue place in the shipp: (Viz) Richard Wooddis<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-090" href="#sec-07-fn-090" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-090" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> pursar, Sophony Cosuke<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-091" href="#sec-07-fn-091" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-091" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> pursers mate, &amp; Edward Nettles<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-092" href="#sec-07-fn-092" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-092" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> Steward, yett we haue interteyned <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-381" data-page="381" data-folio="289" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n380_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 289">[p. 289]</button> them to serue vs vij yeares, there w<sup>th</sup> you, &amp; would haue them ymployed in o<sup>r</sup> busines &amp; such other p̱sonns, as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fitt sent backe in the shipp in their place. ffurther we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to Consider w<sup>th</sup> your selues &amp; thereof w<sup>th</sup> the next, advise vs, if it be not more convenient to procure Cloues, Nutmegge, mace, Synamon, Camfere, Benianyn, S<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-093" href="#sec-07-fn-093" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-093" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> redd &amp; yellow to be brought from the molloccos, Banda &amp; other places in Iuncke of Bantam, &amp; other the Islande, to yo<sup>u</sup>, &amp; yo<sup>u</sup> thereto by<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-094" href="#sec-07-fn-094" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-094" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> them, &amp; lay the same in yo<sup>r</sup> warehowse, to be reddie Clensed &amp; fitted for o<sup>r</sup> shipps, when they shall theare arive, Then<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-095" href="#sec-07-fn-095" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-095" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> to send o<sup>r</sup> ships to the places to fetch them, In w<sup>ch</sup> Considerac̃on yo<sup>u</sup> are to remember, to thinke on the price they may be had at, beinge soe brought to Bantam of the Custome and chardge there, of the saffetie of the place, from the invasion of the Portugalls w<sup>ch</sup> yt is thought they will assaye by Comand of the kinge of Spaigne (haueing now peace w<sup>th</sup> the Hollenders<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-096" href="#sec-07-fn-096" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-096" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup>) wherew<sup>th</sup> yt shall not be amisse to acquainte that kinge, that he may endevor to prevent yt: We thinke yf this could be brought to passe much hazard of o<sup>r</sup> shipps and men in comeinge to those parte may be prevented, o<sup>r</sup> voyadge shortened, to o<sup>r</sup> greate ease in victualls and mens wages, &amp; the people of the Country better Contented in that their meanes of liveinge is not taken from them, for w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> write they hate the Hollenders and much bewaile their owne hindrance. And as yo<sup>u</sup> haue sent vs a Iournall of yo<sup>r</sup> accompte, we desire in like manner to haue a Iournall p̱fectlie drawen out, and sent vs by this shipp of all o<sup>r</sup> busines, as the same hath beene passed there, from S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancasters comeinge thence, vntill the begininge of yo<sup>r</sup> chardge, aswell of William Starkey his accompte, w<sup>th</sup> the ballance thereof, deliuered to Edmund Scott, As alsoe a p̱fect Iournall of Edmund Scotte accompte, and the ballance thereof deliu<sup>r</sup>ed over to Gabriell Towerson &amp; the same carefullie pvsed, That entrance thereof may be made into o<sup>r</sup> Booke accordinglie.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-382"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="382" data-folio="290" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n381_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 290">[p. 290]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">money and goodes sent by Capt: David Middleton.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">This voyadge to be a particular acct: aparte from the rest &amp;c:</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Nowe yo<sup>u</sup> shall vnderstand that vpon the hopes we haue of better and more ample trade in those parte: we haue resolued yearelie by Gode pmission and help, to send some shippinge vnto yo<sup>u</sup>: and for that cause not being fitted w<sup>th</sup> a better at this present haue furnished and sent out this o<sup>r</sup> shipp called the Expedic̃on of the burthen of 240 tonnes, whereof Edmund Musgraue is m<sup>r</sup> and Iohn Davies<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-097" href="#sec-07-fn-097" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-097" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Pilott, And therein haue laden 13 ballette cont 41 Clo: 40 devonshire karsayes. 12 hamshire ke. 10 Northerne karseys: &amp; 46 tonns of Iron the nomber sorte Cullers and prices, being expressed in the Invoise hereinclosed, alsoe Chests Cont Royalls of 8. All w<sup>ch</sup> money and goode we haue appointed Captaine David Middleton w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> Councell, advise, and assistaunce to make sale of, and ymploy for vs, either at the Molloccos, <em>Banda</em>, <em>Bantam</em>, or els wheare, as in his &amp; yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̃ons shall seeme fitt for o<sup>r</sup> most benefitt, Consideringe what sorte and quantities of those East Indian m<sup>r</sup>chandize our other shipps haue laden there whome we haue appointed cheefe Comander in the said Shipp, and for whose assistaunce we would haue the aforesaid Thomas Emmesworth Henry Sydall and Augustine Spaldinge to accompany &amp; assist him, for the better pformance of the ladinge thereof, either at the Molluccos Banda or ellswhere, as yo<sup>u</sup> shall resolue: And the said Emseworth or Siddall to write and send vs a pfect accompt, of the whole pceedinge therein, and this further we advise yo<sup>u</sup>, That <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-383" data-page="383" data-folio="291" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n382_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 291">[p. 291]</button> whatsoeur shall be either sould or bought for this shipps ladinge must be done as a pticular stocke a pte from the rest of o<sup>r</sup> busines Soe as yf there be any Cause to vse any of o<sup>r</sup> goode w<sup>ch</sup> are there belonginge to o<sup>r</sup> generall accompt the same is to be deliuered to this stocke, by weight, measure, or tale, And the same Valewed as yt is there worth: In like manner yf any thinge of the goode sent in this Shipp shall be left ther vnsould, the same in like case to be valued as yt is there then worth, &amp; a note of all the said quantities &amp; valua<span class="sc">c</span>õns to be sent to vs by this Shipp, That o<sup>r</sup> accompte there may be rightlie chardged therewith and a due repti<span class="sc">c</span>õn made accordinglie: And for that we vnderstand Augustine Spaldinge behaueth him selfe both honestlie and dilligentlie in matters comitted to his chardge for o<sup>r</sup> occasions, we are contented to increase his wages from what he formerlie had, to xl<sup>s</sup> p monneth (yf he be liueinge) The same wages to beginn at the arriuall of this Shipp att Bantam, not doubtinge but that in his honest and faithfull carriadge towarde vs, he will deserue the same.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Tho: Hemsworth.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">ffurther concerninge Thomas Emmseworth o<sup>r</sup> desire is that the shipp beinge laden and retourned from the Molluccoes Banda or elswheare to Bantam: That he stay w<sup>th</sup> you in o<sup>r</sup> howse there, and take chardge of the passinge of o<sup>r</sup> busines to accompt, being assisted and acquainted w<sup>th</sup> the manner of m<sup>r</sup>chandizeinge there, by some of you best acquainted therewith: And that yearelie we may receiue a true Iournall of what hath passed in o<sup>r</sup> affaires there, by you, Soe as we may knowe trulie the pfect state thereof: we have alsoe sent you herewith, the Coppies of o<sup>r</sup> <span class="sc">l</span>res formerlie sent you, aswell by m<sup>r</sup> Keelinge, as by m<sup>r</sup> Sharpey o<sup>r</sup> twoe Generalls in the third and fourth Voyadge: And we wish you, That yf you cann, you doe lade and send vs any other Comodities rather then pepp, Yett rather then the shipp shall wante or retourne w<sup>th</sup>out ladinge, Then to send vs some pepp. w<sup>ch</sup> is more at lardge specified in o<sup>r</sup> Comission for the Captaine.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Lastlie forasmuch as we haue vnderstoode, That Gabriell Towerson hath a greate desier to retourne home, we are Contented that he soe doe: hopinge that he hath not soe lightlie reguarded the trust reposed in him, That he would abandon the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-384" data-page="384" data-folio="292" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n383_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 292">[p. 292]</button> place, before o<sup>r</sup> leaue and direccõn, neither doe we thinke that any of yo<sup>u</sup> all will soe lightlie reguard either yo<sup>r</sup> reputacõn in the world, or yo<sup>r</sup> Creditt w<sup>th</sup> vs, That yo<sup>u</sup> will leaue yo<sup>r</sup> place of ymployment, before we giue o<sup>r</sup> Consent, The like leaue we alsoe giue to Henry Siddall to returne for England, if he may be spared, wherein we wish you to haue reguard to keepe a convenient nomber in o<sup>r</sup> howse both for the defence of yo<sup>r</sup> selues and o<sup>r</sup> goode, as to giue releefe and supplie to such ffactories, as haue beene setled by m<sup>r</sup> William Keelinge in any other place thereaboute: And whereas we finde by yo<sup>r</sup> accompte, That yo<sup>u</sup> receiue yo<sup>r</sup> sallarie or wages at iiij<sup>s</sup> the Ryall of: 8: and soe pay yo<sup>r</sup> selues, we haue thought good to lett yo<sup>u</sup> knowe that we pay for the same 4<sup>s</sup> 7<sup>d</sup> beside the adventure thither: wherefore we thinke yt fitt That yf yo<sup>u</sup> receiue yo<sup>r</sup> wages there fforasmuch as we Vnderstand that they are worth there: 8<sup>s</sup>: That yo<sup>u</sup> sett them at such an indifferent rate as may neither be too lowe for vs, nor too high for yo<sup>r</sup> selues, whereof by yo<sup>r</sup> next letters we desire to receiue some satisfaccõn: <em>Alsoe</em> we vnderstand that yo<sup>u</sup> make greate benefitt by Casshes and dealinge there w<sup>th</sup> other Comodities &amp; yett we finde nothinge putt to o<sup>r</sup> accompte in that respect.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Wherefore we could wish and hereby requier yo<sup>u</sup>, That such a course may be taken w<sup>th</sup> the stocke that remaines in yo<sup>r</sup> hande from tyme to tyme, That thereby a proffitt may be made thereof towarde the greate chardge of howsekeepinge &amp; otherwyse expended there: whereof we make noe doubte but yo<sup>u</sup> will be carefull, And soe Comitt yo<sup>u</sup> to the proteccõn of Thalmightie. London the xv<sup>th</sup> of Aprill 1609</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> loueinge freinde Thomas Smyth William Romeny William Greenwell Robert Bell Robert Middleton W<sup>m</sup>: Cater Iohn Busbridge.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-385"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="385" data-folio="293" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n384_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 293">[p. 293]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Edmond Scott and George Scott Citizens and grocers of London bound vnto S<sup>r</sup> Tho: Smyth of London knight in 500<sup>li</sup> to be paid vnto him, or his certeine Atturney, Executors or Administrators ad vsum Gubnator et Societatis m<sup>r</sup>cator Londinensiū ad Indias Orientales negotiantiū. That the said Edmond Scott his Executors Administrators &amp; Assignes doe stand to the arbitram<sup>t</sup> of those mentioned in this Condic̃on of awarde: And S<sup>r</sup> Tho: Smyth bound in the like some That the w<sup>th</sup>in named Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Company of m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London tradinge into theast Indies &amp; their Successors shall doe the like.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-098" href="#sec-07-fn-098" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-098" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><figure class="archival-plate" id="fig-016"><img src="/figures/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/fig-016_p0385.webp" alt="Decorative drop capital T." loading="lazy"></figure><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The bond of award betweene the Companie &amp; Edmond Scott. 1609.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">THE Condic̃on of this Obligac̃on is such That yf &amp;c: shall at all tymes heereafter stand to, abide, obay obserue pforme, fulfill, &amp; keepe the award, arbitram<sup>t</sup>, order, determinac̃on &amp; Iudgm<sup>t</sup> of Robt Middleton, Robert Iohnson, Raphe ffreeman and Christopher Cletherowe Citizens &amp; m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London, Arbitrators indifferently named elected &amp; chosen aswell on the pte and behalfe of &amp;c: as on the pte of &amp;c: to arbitrate awarde deeme &amp; iudge betweene the said &amp;c: and the said &amp;c: for touchinge &amp; conc’ninge all accompte Reckonninge debte debate and demande whatsoeur now dependinge betweene the said &amp;c: &amp; the said &amp;c: Except all such seu<sup>r</sup>all Adventures &amp; stocke as the said Edmond hath here adventured w<sup>th</sup> the said Company &amp; the proffitte thereof: Soe as the same awarde determinac̃on &amp; Iudgm<sup>t</sup> of the said Arbitrators be made &amp; yealded vp in writeinge vnder their hande &amp; seales on this side or before the 24<sup>th</sup> day of Iulie next ensueinge the date of this present Obligac̃on w<sup>th</sup>out fraude or covyn. That then this present Obligac̃on shalbe &amp;c: Or ells &amp;c:</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-386"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="386" data-folio="294" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n385_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 294">[p. 294]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ILLS of Adventure<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-099" href="#sec-07-fn-099" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-099" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> giuen by the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor &amp; Comp: of East India Marchante to the seu<sup>r</sup>all Adventurers in the 4<sup>th</sup> Voyadge w<sup>th</sup> their seuerall somes, &amp; the same vnderwritten by Richard Atkinson<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-100" href="#sec-07-fn-100" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-100" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> as by him examined, before the ensealeinge thereof. The w<sup>ch</sup> are as ffolloweth. Viz to.—</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To m<sup>r</sup>.</aside></div><div class="ledger-table-block" id="tbl-015"><div class="row-data"><td>Roger</td><td>Clarke</td><td class="num">550 . 0 .</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Iohn S<sup>t</sup></td><td>Andrew</td><td class="num">200 . 0 .</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">In the name of God Amen the 14<sup>th</sup> day of Aprill Anno dn̄i 1609. A Comission agreed vpon and sett downe by us the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> Deputy and Comittees of the Marchaunt<em>e</em> of London tradeinge to the East Indies, <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-388" data-page="388" data-folio="296" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n387_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 296">[p. 296]</button> aswell for the better direccoñ of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind, and principall ffactor Captaine David Middleton, As for all other ffacto<sup>rs</sup>, Master, Pylott, Pursers Mariners, and other Officers or psonns goeinge in this o<sup>r</sup> present intended Voyadge to the East Indies in the good shipp named the Expedicoñ: w<sup>ch</sup> we humbly beseech Almightie God, to blesse w<sup>th</sup> a happy successe and Conclucoñ.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-102" href="#sec-07-fn-102" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-102" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><strong>1</strong></aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Nprimis</span> whereas we the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> Deputie Comittees &amp; Marchaunte aforesaid have to o<sup>r</sup> greate chardge prepared the said shipp named the Expedicoñ of the burthen of 240 Tonnes, wherein Edmund Musgraue goeth m<sup>r</sup> for a voyadge by Godę grace to be made to the East Indies, And haue furnished the same sufficientlie and in plentifull manner, w<sup>th</sup> men, tackle, victualles and all other needfull puisions for such a voyadge, of a spetiall trust and Confidence, w<sup>ch</sup> we haue in the integritie and iust carriadge of o<sup>r</sup> said loueinge freinde David Middleton; we doe appointe and authorize him Captaine to goe in the same shipp, and to Comand all the menn therein, haueinge pcured him sufficient authoritie from o<sup>r</sup> Sou<sup>r</sup>eigne lord the kinge mat<sup>ie</sup>: in that behalfe: Not doubtinge but that he will soe behaue himselfe, as he may be both feared and loued. And that he will w<sup>th</sup> all his dilligence and endeavors prosequute o<sup>r</sup> designes &amp; labour to bringe this o<sup>r</sup> voyadge to a happie end.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">2 Item for that Religious gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> and exercise, doth best bynde menn to pforme their Duties: We doe appointe and order: That prayers be said Morneinge &amp; eveninge in the shipp, And the whole Company called therevnto, That all ioinctlie may w<sup>th</sup> reverence &amp; humility pray vnto Almightie God to blesse &amp; preserue them from all dangers in this longe and tedious voyadge: And for better pformance thereof we haue deliu<sup>r</sup>ed a Bible vnto the Pursar.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">3 Item that noe blasphemeinge of God sweareinge thefte drunckennes or other lyke misdemeanors be vsed, But that the same be sevearelie punnished, And that noe diceinge, or other unlawfull games be pmitted, ffor that the same ar most <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-389" data-page="389" data-folio="297" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n388_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 297">[p. 297]</button> comolie the begyninge of quarrells &amp; many tymes murther a iust occasion of Godę wrath and vengeance from w<sup>ch</sup> the Lord deliver vs all: wherefore we doe order That there shall be ponishmente prouided sett vpp and published in the said shipp for such Offences, whereby eu<sup>r</sup>y man may take notice what he ought to avoied.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">4 Item That noe liquor be spilt in the Ballast of the shipp or filthines be left w<sup>th</sup>in bourde, w<sup>ch</sup> in heate breadeth noysome smells and infecc̃õn: But that the overlopps and other place of the shipp, be kept cleane and sweete w<sup>ch</sup> is a notable preservac̃õn of health.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">5 Item That Contynuall and true Iournalls, be kept of eu<sup>r</sup>y daies course, and Navigac̃õn duringe the whole voyadge, w<sup>th</sup> a true relac̃õn of eu<sup>r</sup>y thinge that passeth: And this to be done seu<sup>r</sup>ally by Captaine, Master, Pilott, the Marchaunt Masters mate and pursar and they at Convenient tymes to Conferr togither aboute the same, To thend that yf any of them haue forgotten what an other hath obserued the same may be added whereby a pfect discourse may be sett downe to be presented to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Company when God shall graunte the said shipp safe retourne and to be kept for better direcc̃õn of posteritie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">6 Item That the Company &amp; M<sup>r</sup> take due notice of the seu<sup>r</sup>all provisions, deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to the said shipp, and to settle the same in the chardge of the Officers appointed for that purpose. And that the same be entred in the Pursars booke vnderwritten by the said Officers, To thend that such thingę as are puided, for their needfull supplies, may not be ymbecilled, And that nothinge thereof, be ymployed for any vse whatsoeuer, w<sup>th</sup>out warrant vnder the hand of the Captaine, directed to the Officer that hath such chardge w<sup>ch</sup> warrant shall serue for his dischardge in his accompte otherwyse to be awnswearable for the same w<sup>th</sup> his wagę, And eu<sup>r</sup>y such warrant to be registred in the pursars booke, The day of the monneth &amp; the occasion, And this to be done alsoe by the Pursar vpon forfecture of soe much out of his wages.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">7 Item That at eu<sup>r</sup>y place where yo<sup>u</sup> shall water and refresh yo<sup>r</sup> selues yo<sup>u</sup> shall call the whole Company together giueinge them most stricte &amp; straight chardge and warneinge not to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-390" data-page="390" data-folio="298" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n389_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 298">[p. 298]</button> straggle but to behaue them selues peaceably &amp; Civillie towarde the people of that place (yf any be there) the better to <span class="sc">p</span>cure their freindshipp towarde the supply of yo<sup>r</sup> wante, And the like to be done and vsed, in eu<sup>r</sup>y place where yo<sup>u</sup> come, least the losse of yo<sup>r</sup> liues and ou<sup>r</sup>throwe of o<sup>r</sup> voyadge, pay for yo<sup>r</sup> disorders, beside an vtter discredditt to o<sup>r</sup> Nac̃õn: And that yo<sup>r</sup> men doe vse, a discreete meane in eateinge of fruite, fresh victualls, or drinkeinge of water least they surfett, and fall into diseases, whereof we haue had too much experience: And the better to restraine vnrulie appetite: We thinke fit That yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine doe appointe some of the discreetest in yo<sup>r</sup> Company to <span class="sc">p</span>cure and prouide in such places, what yo<sup>u</sup> want: And the same to be first brought vnto yo<sup>u</sup>, and yo<sup>u</sup> to repte the same to yo<sup>r</sup> Company as yo<sup>u</sup> shall see good and needfull, And that none presume to doe contrary w<sup>th</sup>out seveare ponishment: Alsoe that yo<sup>u</sup> haue a spetiall care, to see the sicke releeued w<sup>th</sup> such fresh meate and other Comfortable thinges, wherew<sup>th</sup> we haue furnished the said shipp for that purpose: And that the same be not otherwyse spent in Ryott &amp; banquettinge, and soe the sicke <span class="sc">p</span>ishe for want of thinge needfull.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">8. Item when yo<sup>u</sup> haue refreshed yo<sup>r</sup> selues at Saldania or elswhere thereaboute, where yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke most convenient and good for harbor, &amp; plentie of victualls: yo<sup>u</sup> shall apply yo<sup>r</sup> selues, and w<sup>th</sup> all possible speede endevour yo<sup>r</sup> course for Bantam, And being arriued there, yo<sup>u</sup> shall Conferr and take aduise of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors for or Conc<sup>r</sup>ninge yo<sup>r</sup> voyadge to the Molluccos or Banda: And shall take alonge w<sup>th</sup> you thither such of o<sup>r</sup> said ffactors there as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke good, espetially Syddall for his experience as alsoe Spaldinge whome we hould the fittest psonns in that behalfe: But yf that yo<sup>u</sup> cannott <span class="sc">p</span>cure yo<sup>r</sup> ladeinge at the Molluccoes and Banda: Then we leaue the same to yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̃õn, to be done at Bantam, or any other place where yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke will be most for o<sup>r</sup> proffitte.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">9 Item yo<sup>u</sup> shall espetiallie foresee, and take care, That in ladeinge either Cloues Nutmegge or Mace yo<sup>u</sup> cleanes them as much as yo<sup>u</sup> cann from dust and Rumps, That the shipp be not stuffed w<sup>th</sup> such vnproffitable ware as is not worth the Custome, And soe we loose all o<sup>r</sup> chardge: wherefore rather burne the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-391" data-page="391" data-folio="299" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n390_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 299">[p. 299]</button> same, then<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-103" href="#sec-07-fn-103" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-103" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> lade it in the shipp And be carefull to buy dry Cloues and not such as are greene.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">10 Item That yo<sup>u</sup> be very carefull to haue yo<sup>r</sup> due and full weight and the same putt into bagge nombred and marked and sent abourd the shipp w<sup>th</sup> some ffactor or other trustie psonn, w<sup>th</sup> a łre Conteyneinge the pticular of eu<sup>r</sup>y bagge vnder the nomber, The same łre to be deliuered to the pursar, whome we chardge to weigh eu<sup>r</sup>y bagge, at the takeing of yt into the shipp, whereby he may see that the weight is accordinge to the łre receiued, keepinge eu<sup>r</sup>y łre and entringe the Contente in his booke whereby yf any error happen yt may be found out, w<sup>ch</sup> we wish yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine to examyne eu<sup>r</sup>y nighte That there growe noe abuse.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">11 Item That the purser be Contynually aboorde the shipp and take true notice of all thinge whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> are brought into the shipp or carried out eyther by the Captaine Marchaunt ffactor Master Mariner, the pursar himselfe or any other whosoeuer, And to enter the same in his booke vnder the name of the Owner that eu<sup>r</sup>y one may haue his due at the returne, As the pursar will awnsweare yt w<sup>th</sup> the losse of his wage: And yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine to giue the pursar stricte chardge in this behalfe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">12 Item, That there be but one hatch open (yf yt may be) at the ladeinge of the shipp, and thother hatches to be locked vp, and that the master or his Mate and the purser to stand by the open hatche when any goode are shott into hould lockeinge the same vpp when they haue left worke, and the key to be giuen to the Capteine to keepe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">13 Item That nothinge be bought, but what is espetiall good And that yo<sup>u</sup> doe not overwett the Cloues,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-104" href="#sec-07-fn-104" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-104" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> badd nutmegge and mace haue taught vs to advise o<sup>r</sup> ffactors to be more carefull.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">14 Item, for that voyadge of this Condic̃on and chardge cannott admitt any priuate trade our will is, And we doe expreslie order That neither yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine, nor any other, vpon any pticuler, or other accompte, then the generall and ioincte stocke doe lade, or deale w<sup>th</sup> any of the Comodities herevnder expressed (Vizt) pepp, Cloues, Nutmegge, Mace, Synamon, Ginger Indico Callicoes Ambergreece, Muske Alloes Beniamyn Gumlacka Rawe &amp; sleeue silke Pearles Dyamonde <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-392" data-page="392" data-folio="300" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n391_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 300">[p. 300]</button> Bezar stones: And that yf any shall soe doe the same shall be applyed to thaccompt of the Company as o<sup>r</sup> owne propꝑ goode  It beinge noe way reasonable That wee should pay them their wages w<sup>th</sup> meate and drinke, And they lade those thinge in o<sup>r</sup> shipp, w<sup>ch</sup> should defray o<sup>r</sup> chardge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">15. Item That in eu<sup>r</sup>y place where yo<sup>u</sup> come and trade yo<sup>u</sup> shall compare their waighte &amp; measures w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>rs</sup> before yo<sup>u</sup> buye To thend yo<sup>u</sup> may knowe the Better what to doe And howe to make yo<sup>r</sup> rekeninge, ffor w<sup>ch</sup> purpose we haue putt a Chest w<sup>th</sup> weighte into our said Shippe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">16. Item we straightlie chardge That the best and sufficientest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors be appointed to wey, what is bought by weight on the land  And to keepe a p̄fect Booke thereof that the same may be nightlie conferred w<sup>th</sup> the pursars booke, whereby errors or abuses may be the better found out.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">17  Item we straightlie chardge that noe excessiue drinkeinge feastinge or banquettinge, or shooteinge of of Gonnes be at any tyme made or vsed either aboorde the Shipp att Sea, or on land, The same beinge a greate waste and Consumpc̃on of victualls, wyne and powder &amp; the cause of many diseases &amp; disorders in the Company  And therefore to be spent w<sup>th</sup> discrec̃on and spareinge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">18  Item That yo<sup>u</sup> matt the inside of the shipp before yo<sup>u</sup> put or lade any spice in her, espetiallie where yo<sup>u</sup> lade Cloues Pepp Nutmegge &amp; Mace, And that yo<sup>u</sup> make choise of such Nutmegge, as be lardge and sounde, And att the ladeinge thereof not to lyme them too much, for that doth burne them,  Alsoe that in any Case, yo<sup>u</sup> lay the Mace in Canisters in some fitt place by yt selfe, That yt be not spoyled by the heate of the other spice, takeinge good heede to buy that w<sup>ch</sup> is bright, &amp; not wythered nor red, nor darke browne Colored  And lykewyse for Cloues to buy such as shall be free of dust, The w<sup>ch</sup> those people doe vse to putt amongst them for their advantage in the waight.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">19  Item That yf necessitie drawe yo<sup>u</sup> at the ladeinge of any Cloues for better setlinge of them, to cast water vpon them, lett yt be but asprinkelinge w<sup>th</sup> some thinge for the purpose like a wateringe pott, for that too much wett in o<sup>r</sup> Cloues in other voyadge, hath brought vs greate damadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">20  Item That yf o<sup>r</sup> ould Cloth w<sup>ch</sup> was left at Bantam, be <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-393" data-page="393" data-folio="301" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n392_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 301">[p. 301]</button> not sould, Then yo<sup>u</sup> shall Conferr and aduyse w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> ffactors there howe the same shall or may be vented, either by sendinge yt to Banda: or by leaueinge (yf neede be) a ffactory at Banda w<sup>th</sup> the same and addinge therevnto some money and some other Comodities as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fitt.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">21 Item That yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine doe giue often notice to all ꝑsons goeinge w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> in the shipp, what they are to obserue by this o<sup>r</sup> Comission &amp; direcc̃ons, And that yo<sup>u</sup> giue espetiall chardge to all o<sup>r</sup> ffactors. That they make diligent inquiry what Comodities are to be bought or bartered for in eu<sup>r</sup>y Island, or place where they shall come or arriue And what English m<sup>r</sup>chandize may be there vented And of what kinde &amp; Color most vendible</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">22 Item That yo<sup>u</sup> remember, aduise &amp; putt o<sup>r</sup> ffactors at Bantam in mynde that they doe their best endevo<sup>rs</sup> to procure the Chineses to bringe from Chiney sleve and soweinge silkes of the Colo<sup>r</sup> and sizes we haue now deliu<sup>r</sup>ed vnto yo<sup>u</sup> As alsoe some Rawe silke that we may fall into some trade w<sup>th</sup> them, And see yf they can sell any of our English Cloth vnto them whereby to bringe them to the vse thereof.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">23 Item, That yo<sup>u</sup> remember to doe yo<sup>r</sup> best endevo<sup>r</sup> to bringe for the Lo: Thr̄er some parratte Munkeys Marmesates, or other strange beaste and fowles, That yo<sup>u</sup> esteeme rare and delightfull.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-105" href="#sec-07-fn-105" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-105" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">24 Item, That for the quantities of moneys &amp; m<sup>r</sup>chandize putt into this our said shipp, we referr yo<sup>u</sup> to o<sup>r</sup> invoise deliu<sup>r</sup>ed vnto yo<sup>u</sup> herew<sup>th</sup>, togither w<sup>th</sup> the ꝑticular sorte Collors and prices, w<sup>th</sup> patternes of all sorte, whereby to make sale.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">25 Item whereas heretofore the Company haue receiued greate wronge by the Generalls Captaines Masters and Mariners admittance of voluntary men to goe in o<sup>r</sup> shipps: We therefore hereby straightlie chardge yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine Master &amp; other Officers, That yo<sup>u</sup> suffer none such to goe alonge w<sup>th</sup> you w<sup>ch</sup> putt the Company to greate chardge beside many other Inconvenience, Nor any but such as are, or shall be allowed of by o<sup>r</sup> direcc̃on and whose names are entered into o<sup>r</sup> Booke.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">26 Item, for that yt is vnfitt, and inconvenient, That o<sup>r</sup> trade <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-394" data-page="394" data-folio="302" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n393_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 302">[p. 302]</button> be ympted vnto any, but to o<sup>r</sup> generall Company we doe hereby expreslie order &amp; appointe, That yo<sup>u</sup> chardge all o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup>, not to write or aduise any their pticular freinde or others of the prices of any Comodities, either there bought or sould But onelie in their generall łres to the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor and Company.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">27   Item That yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine at yo<sup>r</sup> returne from Bantam for England doe bringe w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> vnto vs from thence an Inventory of all o<sup>r</sup> goode debte and moneys then remayneinge in those parte. And that yo<sup>u</sup> putt to o<sup>r</sup> generall accompte all guifte presente &amp; gratificačons w<sup>ch</sup> shall be giuen by any forreine prince, potentate or others, to whome we haue hertofore giuen or sent any presente, or to whome there shall be nowe any given.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">28   <em>Item</em>, That by agenerall Consent of yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine ffactor Master Pilott &amp; Mariners, some good order be by yo<sup>u</sup> sett downe in writeinge, soe soone as yo<sup>u</sup> come to Sea, conc<sup>r</sup>ninge the dispotičon of dead mens goode in the voyadge by sale at the maste, or otherwyse for buyeinge, sellinge borroweinge and lendinge one an other, And orderinge what Sallary shall be allowed the pursar, vpon eu<sup>r</sup>y pound, for keepinge accompte thereof to avoied Controu<sup>r</sup>sie and question heereafter, And eu<sup>r</sup>y Mariner and others in the shipp to sett their hand or marke vnto the same order: But yf any ffactor shall happen to dye, Then his goode booke accompte &amp; other thinge are to be safelie preserued and brought home for England.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">29   <em>Item</em>, that for better preservačon of health, yo<sup>u</sup> doe from tyme to tyme, as the water Caske shall be emptied of fresh water, cause the same to be forthw<sup>th</sup> filled againe w<sup>th</sup> salt water for prevenčon of leakeinge &amp; mustines w<sup>ch</sup> must be often shifted.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">30   <em>Item</em>, touchinge the facte Comitted in the Indies by S<sup>r</sup> Edward Michelborne thoughe we knowe o<sup>r</sup> selues to be meerelie innocent therein, yett yf by any meanes, we or o<sup>r</sup> goode should be dampnified thereby, we doe wish yo<sup>u</sup> to seeke to that kinge &amp; Magistrate for redresse &amp; recompence thereof, The w<sup>ch</sup> yf you cannot obtaine, Then o<sup>r</sup> will and order is that you vse the benefitt of o<sup>r</sup> kinge ma<sup>te</sup> Comission giuen yo<sup>u</sup> in that behalfe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">31   <em>Item</em>, That yf the said David Middleton Captaine shall happen to dye goeinge outwarde in this present voyadge (w<sup>ch</sup> God forbid) then we will &amp; appointe That Thomas Emmesworth <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-395" data-page="395" data-folio="303" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n394_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 303">[p. 303]</button> m<sup>r</sup>chaunt &amp; ffactor<sup>r</sup> in this present voyadge shall succeede in his place, and be principall Comander in the said shipp vntill shee come to Bantam: And that yf the said David Middleton shall happen to dye at Bantam, That then yo<sup>u</sup> the Master Pilott and other cheefe Officers, w<sup>ch</sup> shall arive att Bantam shall Conferr and advise w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> ffactors at Bantam, w<sup>ch</sup> of them there shall be held &amp; esteemed the fittest psonn to succeede in his place &amp; roome: And therevpon he w<sup>ch</sup> they shall soe thinke fittest, our will is, shall pcure the ladeinge of o<sup>r</sup> said shipp, and retourne as principall Comander therein for England.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">32 Item, for that we finde best proffitt, by the lardge sorte of pepp, both for the prices w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> pay aswell for thone as for thoter, we will that in any case yo<sup>u</sup> buy of the lardgest sorte, And lett the same be putt by yt selfe into the shipp, That yt be not mingled w<sup>th</sup> any other: yett o<sup>r</sup> desire is not to haue any pepp except yt be to saue dead fraight.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">33. Item we doe appointe and expreslie order, That yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine w<sup>th</sup> Thomas Emmesworth, the pursar &amp; such others as yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine shall thinke meete to call vnto yo<sup>u</sup>, doe dulie examyn the dealeinge of all or any of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors at Bantam, either in or for o<sup>r</sup> generall accompte or otherwyse or of all or any of their priuate trade or trades, since their goeinge thither: And that yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall therevpon finde all or any of them, to haue dealt or traded for them selfes, or to haue willfullie or cuningelie abused and wronged vs therein, or to haue demeaned them selues contrary to o<sup>r</sup> expresse order formerly giuen vnto them in that behalfe: Then we will that yo<sup>u</sup> take notice of their misdemeanors, and to admonish them, for reformac̃on thereof, As alsoe to c<sup>r</sup>tifie vnto vs the pticuler abuses by them or any of them Comitted.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">34. Item, Lastlie but w<sup>th</sup> the first to be remembred: We chardge and pray yo<sup>u</sup> all, To haue an espetiall care and reguard: And to haue yo<sup>r</sup> Eyes open to all devises &amp; practices w<sup>ch</sup> may be wrought against yo<sup>u</sup>, or<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-106" href="#sec-07-fn-106" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-106" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> shipp, or goode in eu<sup>r</sup>ry or any place where yo<sup>u</sup> come or shall ariue keepinge a Contynuall watch whereby yo<sup>u</sup> may be ready to prevent any mischeefe intended against you, suspect all howe freindlie soeu<sup>r</sup> they seeme: Be <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-396" data-page="396" data-folio="304" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n395_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 304">[p. 304]</button> ready still and stand vpon yo<sup>r</sup> guarde, Trust not yo<sup>r</sup> saffety to the carelesse attendance of the Comon Mariners whoe will not loose their present pleasures or ease, though the losse of their liues or<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-107" href="#sec-07-fn-107" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-107" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> shipp and goode depend vpon yt: And albeyt we haue sett downe eu<sup>r</sup>y thinge w<sup>ch</sup> we remember &amp; desier to be done in pticular yett yf you the Captaine David Middleton the said Thomas Emmesworth Edmund Musgraue M<sup>r</sup>, Iohn Davis Pilott &amp; Thomas Cleyburne<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-108" href="#sec-07-fn-108" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-108" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> shall vpon due Considerac̃on and Consultac̃on together finde cause to alter any thinge before yo<sup>u</sup> come vnto Bantam w<sup>ch</sup> we haue herein sett downe as aforesaid havinge w<sup>th</sup> good advise resolued therevpon, We referr the same to yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̃ons: And for better assistaunce and more wary pceedinge in all courses, vpon all occac̃ons, &amp; in all o<sup>r</sup> affaires and busines for or conc<sup>r</sup>ninge this present voyadge: We will &amp; appoint the said Thomas Emmesworth (soe longe as he shall contynew w<sup>th</sup> you) and Edmund Musgraue Iohn Davis &amp; Thomas Cleyburne to advise &amp; Consulte w<sup>th</sup> you the Captaine of and aboute all thinge w<sup>ch</sup> shall be needfull therein, whome we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to ymbrace and vse w<sup>th</sup> a loueinge respect &amp; freindly &amp; diligently to hearken to their Councells puttinge in execution, what shall w<sup>th</sup> good deliberac̃on be soe, by yo<sup>u</sup> &amp; them agreed vpon together, ffor the better avoiedinge of such Contenc̃ons as were the last voyadge in the Consent. And thus not doubtinge of yo<sup>r</sup> faithfull care &amp; diligence herein: we comend yo<sup>u</sup> and your endevo<sup>rs</sup> to Gode providence, whome we hartilie pray to blesse you all &amp; o<sup>r</sup> affaires, And to guide yo<sup>u</sup> w<sup>th</sup> his feare &amp; defend yo<sup>u</sup> from all dangers <em>Amen.</em> London the 14<sup>th</sup> day of Aprill 1609.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">p me David Middleton</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Sealed &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed in the p<sup>r</sup>sence of Richard Mountney<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-109" href="#sec-07-fn-109" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-109" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Richard Atkinson.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-397"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="397" data-folio="305" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n396_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 305">[p. 305]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">To o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freinde m<sup>r</sup> W<sup>m</sup> Kealinge, m<sup>r</sup> W<sup>m</sup>: Hawkins &amp; to all other o<sup>r</sup> Officers &amp; servante in any o<sup>r</sup> Ships Comeinge out of the East Indies.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-110" href="#sec-07-fn-110" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-110" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><figure class="archival-plate" id="fig-017"><img src="/figures/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/fig-017_p0397.webp" alt="Decorated woodcut initial A with leafy foliage." loading="lazy"></figure><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">4 of this Tenor deliured to m<sup>r</sup> Worstenholme</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER o<sup>r</sup> hartie Comendations &amp;c: Whereas the Officers of his ma<sup>t e</sup> Customes doe vsuallie send some of their servaunte abourde all shipps comeinge to any pte of this Realme to haue care that by noe meanes his ma<sup>t e</sup> Customes may be defrauded, to avoyde all blame to o<sup>r</sup> selues and yo<sup>u</sup>, we haue thought good to requier yo<sup>u</sup> that yf any of their said servante shall come abourde of any of ou Shipps that yo<sup>u</sup> doe intertaine them in kinde and loueing manner for the better execution of their place &amp; his ma<sup>t e</sup> service. And further accordinge to o<sup>r</sup> instruccõns formerlie giuen yo<sup>u</sup>, we earnestly wish &amp; requier yo<sup>u</sup> carefullie to keepe and reserue for his ma<sup>tie</sup>: &amp; the Lorde all such rare fowles beaste or other thinge as are by you, or any of yo<sup>r</sup> Company brought from those pte, and to vse such meanes as none of them be sould or giuen away before they come to o<sup>r</sup> hande, for the comeinge thereof to the hande of his ma<sup>tie</sup>: or the Councell or any other by any other meanes then from vs, will be some disgrace &amp; discontent to the Company. And thus not doubtinge of yo<sup>r</sup> care herein we bid you farewell London the 14<sup>th</sup> of August 1609.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> loueing freinde the Company of Marchaunte of London tradeinge to the East Indies.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-398"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="398" data-folio="306" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n397_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 306">[p. 306]</button></div><h3 class="sec-h2">To o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freinde Anthony Marlowe &amp; ffrancis Bucke or either of them at Lisborne<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-111" href="#sec-07-fn-111" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-111" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></h3><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">one of this Tenor deliured to m<sup>r</sup> Eldred</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER o<sup>r</sup> hartie Comendac̃ons &amp;c: yo<sup>r</sup> łres dated in Lisborne the 15<sup>th</sup> of Iulie last we haue receiued whereby as we are verrie sorrie to heare of yo<sup>r</sup> hard mishapps, soe we haue done &amp; will vse all possible and speedie meanes that in vs lieth for yo<sup>r</sup> releefe and inlardgm<sup>t</sup>, &amp; will be readie to pforme what other thinge yo<sup>u</sup> doe requier of vs by yo<sup>r</sup> said łres: In the meane season we haue thought good by this occasion not onelie to make knowne vnto yo<sup>u</sup> o<sup>r</sup> greefe for this yo<sup>r</sup> distresse, but alsoe to advertice yo<sup>u</sup> that for yo<sup>r</sup> better releefe &amp; Comfort we haue dealt w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> good freind m<sup>r</sup> Iohn Eldred,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-112" href="#sec-07-fn-112" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-112" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> to giue order to his servant W<sup>m</sup>: Squier remayneinge at Lisborne, to furnish yo<sup>u</sup> for yo<sup>r</sup> present necessities w<sup>th</sup> the some of Twoe hundred Duckette yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall need soe much &amp; further (yf need be) w<sup>th</sup> the some of three or fower hundred Duckette more, yf for the same yo<sup>u</sup> cann assuredlie by any good meanes there, worke yo<sup>r</sup> inlardgm<sup>t</sup>: Wherein yo<sup>u</sup> are to be well aduiced &amp; be of some c<sup>r</sup>teine grownde before yo<sup>u</sup> parte w<sup>th</sup> any money &amp; thus wishinge yo<sup>u</sup> to Comforte yo<sup>r</sup> selues in the lord whoe will bringe theis yo<sup>r</sup> troubles to a blessed yssewe in his good tyme as may make most to his glorie &amp; yo<sup>r</sup> Comforte in hast we end as we begunn the 14th of August 1609. Yo<sup>r</sup> loueing freinde the Company of Marchaunte of London tradeinge to the East Indies.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-399"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="399" data-folio="307" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n398_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 307">[p. 307]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">To the right wo<sup>ll</sup>: my very loueinge freinde S<sup>r</sup>: Thomas Walsingham knight &amp; other his Ma<sup>te</sup>: Iustices of the Peace Assembled at Crayford in Kent or elswheare.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-113" href="#sec-07-fn-113" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-113" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AFTER my hartiest Comendacõns &amp;c: Wheareas Anthonie Emerson and [ <em>blank</em> ] shipwrighte latelie hired by the East India Company &amp; by them ymployed about the builedinge of their Shipps at Deptford, doe notw<sup>th</sup>standinge their said agreem<sup>t</sup> now absent them selues from the said worke to the greate hindraunce thereof, and evill example vnto others. I ame in the name of the said Companie to intreate yo<sup>r</sup> favo<sup>rs</sup> herein, espetiallie beinge a Case of greate ymportaunce for the hono<sup>r</sup> and proffitt of this kingdome Whereby they may be soe ponished accordinge to the statute in that respect puided, as yt be a warninge vnto them and others to abstaine from the like. And thus not doubtinge of yo<sup>r</sup> kinde favo<sup>rs</sup> herein I Comitt yo<sup>u</sup> to the Tuicõn of Thalmightie. London the 25<sup>th</sup> of Septemƀ . 1609.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind <em>Tho: Smyth.</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">To the right wo<sup>ll</sup>: my very loueinge freinde S<sup>r</sup> Robert Mansfeild knight one of the principall Officers of his Ma<sup>te</sup>: Navy.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-114" href="#sec-07-fn-114" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-114" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">S<sup>R</sup> The East India Companie haueinge caused certaine Iron Ordinance to be made readie for their shipps now abuildinge at Deptford, haue not as yett any Convenient place for the stowadge thereof. Wherefore I hartelie pray yo<sup>u</sup> to affoorde them soe much favo<sup>r</sup> as to giue yo<sup>r</sup> order that the said Iron Ordinance may be suffered to be laid on his Ma<sup>te</sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-400" data-page="400" data-folio="308" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n399_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 308">[p. 308]</button> Wharfe at Deptford there to remaine for some shorte space vntill the Shipps be lanched. The w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> favo<sup>r</sup> the Companie will be readie to requite in the best manner they May: And I remaine</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> very loueinge freinde</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">London the 25<sup>th</sup> of Septemb̃ 1609.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><em>Tho: Smyth:</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Commission to m<sup>r</sup> Barber to see that nothinge be carried out of the Dragon w<sup>th</sup>out order.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">THEIS<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-115" href="#sec-07-fn-115" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-115" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> are to Certifie all those whome yt may Concerne That we the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie of the Marchaunte of London tradinge into the East Indies haue appointed the Bearer hereof Barber w<sup>th</sup> all expedi<span class="sc">c</span>õn to make his repaier abourd o<sup>r</sup> Shipp called the Dragon latelie come from the Indies, and there to remayne to foresee and vse all meanes for preven<span class="sc">c</span>õn that noe goode of what kinde soever be conveyed or carried out of the said Shipp w<sup>th</sup>out the warrant of the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of the said Company in writeinge accordinge to the Teno<sup>r</sup> of a l̃re from the Lo: highe Thr̃er of England, whearevnto we doe authorize him by this o<sup>r</sup> Comission. In witnes whereof I the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of the said Companie haue hearevnto sett my hand the 15<sup>th</sup> of <em>Nouember</em> 1609.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><em>Tho: Smyth.</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">London the of March 1609.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-116" href="#sec-07-fn-116" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-116" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><span class="sc">Rich</span>: Reaue <span class="sc">W</span>m: Revett Iohn Iourden Phillip Glascocke Ieofferey Carleile <span class="sc">Rich</span>: Weekes and others.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">ALBEYT we haue receiued noe l̃res from yo<sup>u</sup>, nor yo<sup>r</sup> Generall since yo<sup>r</sup> depture, nor otherwyse heard from yo<sup>u</sup>, but by some vncertaine reporte from Cairo, whereby wee vnderstand yo<sup>u</sup> haue traded at Mocho neere Aden, both in sellinge o<sup>r</sup> English and buyinge those <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-401" data-page="401" data-folio="309" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n400_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 309">[p. 309]</button> Country Comodities, whereof we hope yo<sup>u</sup> haue by the Carivan of Aleppo, and by the way of Cairo written vnto vs as alsoe haue sent Iohn Iourden overland accordinge to yo<sup>r</sup> Comission,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-117" href="#sec-07-fn-117" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-117" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> w<sup>ch</sup> yf yo<sup>u</sup> haue neglected yo<sup>u</sup> are worthy much blame, for yt may much preiudice the Company, and be the occasion of yo<sup>r</sup> owne wauntꝭ, for that we knowe not whither to direct either shippinge or lres vnto yo<sup>u</sup>: yett such is the care we haue of yo<sup>r</sup> good that haueinge now sent out three Ships to say the Tradesincrease of 1000 Tuns,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-118" href="#sec-07-fn-118" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-118" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> the Pepp Corne of 240 Tuns and the Little Darlynge of 100 Tuns whereof is Admirall S<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-119" href="#sec-07-fn-119" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-119" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> and m<sup>r</sup> Lawrence ffeemell<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-07-fn-120" href="#sec-07-fn-120" data-fn-target="sec-07-fn-120" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> principall ffactor: we haue not onelie by them sent yo<sup>u</sup> theis lres but alsoe haue giuen them chardge in their Comission, that wheresoever they shall finde any of yo<sup>u</sup> they shewe you all loue and kindnes whereof we doubt not.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Our pleasure therefore is that all such stocke as is (for o<sup>r</sup> accompt of the fourth voyadge) in all or any of yo<sup>r</sup> Custodies or w<sup>ch</sup> you may receiue, yo<sup>u</sup> doe as M<sup>r</sup> ffemell shall aduise yo<sup>u</sup> (yf already yo<sup>u</sup> haue not) speedilie tourne into such Comodities as are best and most proffitable for theis partꝭ, and w<sup>ch</sup> will take least bulke, as good rawe silke, fine booke Callicoes yf they be cheepe, rich Indico, good druggꝭ, cloues, or maces well chosen for we must pay freight for them to the accompt of the Sixt voyadge, yett doe we not soe tye yo<sup>u</sup> to the saveinge of freight but that yo<sup>u</sup> should send such Comodities whereof alsoe wee may make most proffitt, &amp; the same to packe vpp very safe from wettinge markeinge them both vpon the Inner &amp; outer surplus w<sup>th</sup> a distincte marke &amp; n<sup>o</sup>: whereby the same may be distinguished from goodꝭ of any other voyadge.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-402"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="402" data-folio="310" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n401_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 310">[p. 310]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And Concerninge yo<sup>r</sup> selues whether yo<sup>u</sup> shall stay in those parte or not, we haue referred that vnto S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton M<sup>r</sup> ffemell and Captaine Hawkins to deale w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> vpon tearmes of stayinge and to contynue the same wages w<sup>ch</sup> we formerlie agreed w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> for, that soe the chardge of soe many as shall there remaine may be put to the accompt of this Sixt voyadge, and the rest to come home in the first Shipp that retourneth for England. Yo<sup>u</sup> shall alsoe vnderstand that in this Sixt voyadge we giue o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup>, noe liberty for any priuate trade but for soe much as they putt into the generall stocke, wee are contented to accept thereof bindinge them from priuate traffique directly or indirectlie, and for such wages as we are agreed vpon, they or their Assignes shall yearlie receiue the same in England, soe that they are not to receiue any wages in the Indies, exceptinge onlie some x<sup>li</sup> yearelie to buy them apprell, and that to be paid them in Ryalls of 8 at 5<sup>s</sup> ꝑ peece w<sup>ch</sup> we knowe to be a reasonable rate consideringe they cost us here 4<sup>s</sup>—6<sup>d</sup> ꝑ Ryall beside the venture and forbearance.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Now therefore soe many of yo<sup>u</sup> as shall be elected to stay there and shall be contented w<sup>th</sup> theis Condicõns, giueinge Bond for the true ꝑformance thereof shall be assured to haue yo<sup>r</sup> wages according to agreement.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And because yo<sup>u</sup> had some liberty granted by the fourth voyadge to adventure some small matter in priuate we are content that such as stay behinde as ffactor<sup>s</sup> (in payinge freight) shall send home such stocke as they haue gathered, vnto m<sup>r</sup>
